# Agent scratchpad history

AGENTS.md's agent scratchpad as it stood on 2026-11-09, moved here byte for byte that day (dreeves: "ok, yes, decruft AGENTS.md (without touching the humans-only part of course)") so that sessions stop loading all of it. AGENTS.md's scratchpad now holds a short current state. Append dated notes on finished work at the end of this file. Paths under scratch/ exist only on dreeves's Mac.

---

This project began as the claude.ai chat "Partitioning 1-240 with equal power sums" (2026-10-03 to 2026-10-06, Opus 5.5 at max effort). Its prompts and replies were seeded into sourcery.html once, from Chrome's local claude.ai cache. The other files are what the chat produced and dreeves downloaded: overnight_240.py (its final edited version), overnight_240_v2.py, mma240.wl, certificate_2^24.wl, and X_order25_2^24.txt.gz.

Browser checks in Codex (2026-10-20): launching the installed Chrome inside the workspace sandbox aborted with SIGABRT, including on about:blank, matching the user's reports of macOS crash dialogs. The existing Chrome/CDP quals and screenshot harnesses succeeded with the same browser flags when run through an approved `require_escalated` tool call. For those checks, request that execution mode from the outset; do not launch Chrome inside the sandbox or repeat failed launch probes. Keep isolated temporary browser profiles. Do not disable Chrome's own sandbox or change global permissions. Switching to Playwright has not been verified to solve this launch failure.

288:10 search (Astra, 2026-10-20): `lattice288.py` runs 2,736 symmetric cases with one worker and a 30-second wall-time limit per attempt. Its empty and timed-out attempts remain unresolved; finishing this batch cannot establish nonexistence. The production path independently recovered 144:8 and 48:6 controls; 240:10 was checked for exact coset/radius inclusion but was not rediscovered. Run from this directory with `PYTHONPATH=scratch/lower24-design/beyond1000/pylib /opt/homebrew/bin/python3 -B lattice288.py`. The append-only `lattice288_journal.jsonl` supports resuming completed attempts, and an exclusive lock prevents duplicate runners. Preparation is regenerated; `.lattice288_prepared.json` records its output. The journal and workers check a hash of `lattice288.py`, `overnight_v5.py`, and `certbb.py`: inspect running processes before editing those files during a search. The live graph reads this journal through `/opt/homebrew/bin/python3 -B progress.py --watch`; keep only one snapshot writer running. On the initial launch, search output goes to `lattice288.log` and watcher output to `.lattice288_work/progress.log`. These background processes were launched outside the workspace sandbox at niceness 15. They do not publish or push the page.

480:14 and 672:13 searches (Astra, 2026-10-20): `focus.py 480` and `focus.py 672` run resumable proof searches covering all sign assignments. `focus480.py` verifies 496 initial exclusions and reduces the remaining cases to 195 representatives under complement and reflection. `focus672.py` verifies that both prime-residue profiles must have balanced counts modulo 4. These reductions alone prove neither target impossible. Each invocation allows at most 1,000,000 LP solves or one hour, checked between solves; a budget stop remains unresolved. Atomic proof/checkpoint chunks live in `scratch/focus480/run` and `scratch/focus672/run`, with root-level `focus_480_journal.jsonl` and `focus_672_journal.jsonl` feeding the existing progress watcher. Repeating the same command replays saved integer certificates before continuing. The continuation hash covers `focus.py`, `focus480.py`, `focus672.py`, `focus480_certificates.json.gz`, `certbb.py`, `mstar.py`, and `overnight_v5.py`; inspect running processes before editing these files. Launch with the same Python/PYTHONPATH as the 288 search, single-threaded BLAS, and niceness 15. Keep only one `progress.py --watch` process. No completion ETA is inferred from LP or leaf counts.

720:11 joined-witness search (Astra, 2026-10-21): `join720.py` first searches for a 528:10 control by fixing the known 192:9 prefix and solving for an antisymmetric 336:9 suffix. Only an independently rediscovered, exactly verified control unlocks the target: a fixed 336:10 prefix plus a symmetric 384:10 suffix whose tenth binomial moment cancels the prefix's. Four calibration attempts and sixteen target attempts each have a 45-second wall limit covering randomized reduction and pruned enumeration; shared exact preparation is outside that limit. Failure to recover the control stops the run. Empty attempts, timeouts, and a completed batch prove no upper bound. Run with the same Python/PYTHONPATH, single-threaded BLAS, and niceness 15 as above. `join720_journal.jsonl` is resumable; `join720.log` records output, and all prepared/job/lock files stay in the Live Server-excluded `scratch/join720/` directory. The fingerprint covers `join720.py`, `lattice288.py`, `mstar.py`, `overnight_v5.py`, `certbb.py`, and the exact fixed polynomial inputs; inspect running processes before editing those sources. The existing single `progress.py --watch` writer displays calibration and target attempts separately, with no completion ETA. The current watcher log is `scratch/join720/progress.log`. No browser or screen access is authorized for verification.

The initial four calibration timeouts are archived under `scratch/join720/initial-calibration/`. They exposed insufficient precision when recentering a 303-bit affine target: 212-bit MPFR left a residual of squared length approximately 7.712e66; 431-bit MPFR reduced it to 416 in the same exact coset. The corrected worker chooses at least 128 guard bits beyond its largest integer input and asserts the exact nearest-plane bound on the residual norm. This correction passed a new large-target qual before the bounded calibration was restarted. The earlier 288 prepared targets have at most 128 bits. Four subsequent calibration attempts returned empty in about three seconds each; their source, journal, and prepared inputs are archived under `scratch/join720/precision-corrected-calibration/`. The final bounded calibration uses pruning floors 0.3, 0.4, 0.5, and 0.6, and `FIRST_N_SOLUTIONS` stops enumeration at its first candidate, which is then verified exactly. A dense affine qual reduced enumeration work from 439 nodes to 1 with that strategy; this is not a timing claim for the 528 or 720 searches.

The final calibration ended on 2026-10-21 with two empty attempts and two timeouts, without recovering a 528 witness. Its root journal records `control_failed`, and no 720 attempts ran. The bound remains `10 <= m*(720) <= 14`. Repeating the unchanged command does not create new attempts; preserve the completed journal before any deliberately revised experiment. Live Server was not listening on port 5501 during the final verification; generated snapshots updated successfully through a temporary loopback HTTP server, and the existing native watcher qual passed. No screen or browser was accessed.

Residue-conditioned continuation (Astra, 2026-10-21): the preceding run's exact source, journal, log, and prepared input are archived in `scratch/join720/tail-floor-calibration/`. `join720.py` now derives the unique necessary residue profiles modulo 2, 3, and 5, then retains every compatible profile modulo 7. Exact row relations leave seven 528 control cases of dimension 156 and two 720 target cases of dimension 180. No known suffix coordinates or hints enter this construction. One saturated integer kernel and one LLL reduction are shared across each phase's cases; all affine targets and radii are checked exactly. Fourteen calibration attempts cycle through all seven cases twice; sixteen target attempts cycle through both target cases. Each attempt has a 30-second wall limit, including randomized reduction and enumeration. The target phase still requires an independently recovered, exactly verified control. The pruning curve uses the actual nearest-plane tail costs plus a neighboring-coordinate allowance, and a fixed power curve on the remaining coordinates. It is heuristic and establishes no exclusion. The existing 12 runner quals, nine residue quals, and four geometry/scheduling quals pass. No new explicit `if` statements were added; two profile-filter predicates check sign-count feasibility and exact linear consistency. Do not restart a completed batch unchanged or infer upper bounds from it.

The live server on port 5501 was checked through HTTP and its WebSocket after the user's restart; a 70-second observation saw snapshot updates and no reload or refreshcss messages. Keep a single `progress.py --watch` writer, and use HTTP/WebSocket verification only. A scratch fpylll pruning optimizer crashed in double precision; MPFR optimization also exceeded its bounded budget. The production runner does not use that optimizer. The historical 528 recovery time of 0.6 seconds is present in the transcript, but its original seed, settings, source, and raw run log were not recovered; it is not a reproduced benchmark.

Residue-conditioned batch launched (2026-10-21): the root `control_failed` journal was byte-identical to `scratch/join720/tail-floor-calibration/join720_journal.jsonl` and was moved to `scratch/join720/completed-control-failed-journal.jsonl` before the revised runner started. The matching log was moved to `scratch/join720/completed-control-failed.log`. Runner, residue, and geometry quals were green. `quals_join_progress.py` still fails `test_page_has_latin_attributed_progress_and_terminal_states`: neither the current page nor commit `76f20b6` contains `quaestionem apertam`. Attempt 4, case 4, recovered an exactly verified order-10 sequence of length 528 with the fixed 192 prefix, in 18.7 s and 671,529,820 enumeration nodes. It differs in 160 suffix positions from the recorded 336 suffix and is not that suffix negated or reversed. All sixteen 30-second target attempts timed out. The journal finish is `budget`. No 720:11 witness was found. Timeouts establish no exclusion; the bound remains `10 <= m*(720) <= 14`. Repeating the unchanged command creates no new attempts. The single `progress.py --watch` process died on a bare assertion while the root journal was replaced; a one-shot snapshot then included this batch, and one watcher was restarted. Code hash `3579078b6fed57fddbf4f80dfb68e8ba3b4efdb5536e85210dd225d56ee8866a`.

816 order-14 residue probe (2026-10-21): chains through modulus 16 already have admissible vectors. `(5, 25)` in the digits sweep produced a vector within a 20,000,000-node cap in 19.9 s, so that chain does not exclude order 14. `(5, 25)` natural, `(3, 9, 27)`, and `(7, 49)`, in both sweeps, capped at 20,000,000 nodes with no vector and no finish. No upper bound changed. A scratch probe then started two new 720 target seeds, attempts 16 and 17, at a 600-second wall, using the existing prepared search cases and not the production journal. Timeouts still establish no exclusion.

Both 600-second target attempts timed out. Direct enumeration then found an admissible order-14 vector for 816 modulo 27 and for 720 modulo 25, so those moduli do not exclude order 14. Modulo 49 had produced no vector. A scratch probe of that modulus, then modulo 32, was started. A cap still establishes no exclusion.

Focus searches unbounded (Claude, 2026-10-23 13:32 PDT): dreeves approved search-automation principles (no per-run budget; priority = equal value / Lindy median ETA, with preemption; up to 6 slots at nice 15; keep >= 100 GB disk free; new searches need green quals and a positive control). `focus.py 480` and `focus.py 672` now run with `--seconds inf --lp-budget 1000000000000000000`, same environment and niceness as above, until they finish or crash. A restart replays and re-verifies every saved certificate: 92 minutes for 480 at 4.3 GB, about 20 for 672 at 0.6 GB, growing with the checkpoints. progress.py now counts a focus.py process as running from the watcher's first sighting of it to its last (`.progress_cache.json` `_launches`, [first, last] by pid), so reloads show. 432:12 re-derivation (2026-10-23, `scratch/a11/`, quals `quals_profiles.py`): another chat's claims all hold, by finished enumerations or exact checks: mod 5 forces ±(−75,−75,50,50,50) (6 plus signs of 87 in classes 0 and 1), mod 3 and 2 balanced, 24 mod-7 vectors of which Gale's transport test leaves (14,14,14,14,14,−35,−35), mod 25 admits a vector (CP-SAT found it; checked exactly), and the free part's cokernel has torsion 3^11·5^19·7^11 (Mathematica). But `viability.wl` shows the proposed meet-in-the-middle cannot decide 432:12: the 6+6 fixed plus signs reach only a coset of a subgroup of order 5^8, so about 6.5×10^11 of the 2.5×10^17 cases would pass at random (control: the 480:12 witness's own case passes, one swap fails).

Shepherd (Claude, 2026-10-23 13:41 PDT; name approved by dreeves): `shepherd.py` keeps the registered searches (`SEARCHES`: today focus.py 480 and 672, unbounded) running by those principles. Every 60 s it reads the process list, each search's journal and the free disk; it logs each status change to `shepherd.log` (JSON lines), launches searches with no journal or ending in a budget finish, never relaunches one that stopped any other way (crash, kill, reboot), and keeps two floors of free disk (dreeves, 2026-10-23): below 100 GiB nothing launches and the not-done search with the most checkpoint data stops; below 50 GiB every running search stops. It deletes nothing. At about 8 GB a day, 480:14 reaches the first floor around 2026-10-30. It asserts that the searches not done fit 6 slots and 12 GiB; preemption by priority is not built, so a seventh search makes it fail loudly. Run one, from this directory, with `PYTHONPATH=scratch/lower24-design/beyond1000/pylib nohup /opt/homebrew/bin/python3 -B shepherd.py >> shepherd.err 2>&1 &`; `scratch/shepherd.lock` refuses a second. It imports `progress.processes`, read once at its start. A search added to `SEARCHES` needs green quals and a positive control first, and a restart of the shepherd. Quals: `quals_shepherd.py`.

Fanout (Claude, 2026-10-23 16:52 PDT; name approved by dreeves): `fanout.py N` finishes a stopped `focus.py N` run with 5 worker processes. It takes the run's open frontier from its last chunk (`scratch/fanoutN/roots.json.gz`); each job proves a box depth first exactly as focus.py would, checked by `certbb.Check` on that box, for at most 20,000 LP solves, then hands back its open boxes as new jobs. Shallowest jobs run first. Job files under `scratch/fanoutN/jobs/` are the durable record, and a restart reruns none of them. When no job is left, the splice replays the run (which must still be stopped at the same frontier), feeds every job's tokens through `focus.State` in sequential order, commits them as ordinary focus.py chunks, and then calls unchanged `focus.search` to replay the whole run from disk and write its finish. A witness ends the fanout at once and is exactly checked. 672:13's focus.py run was stopped at 2,362,671 LP solves with 89 open boxes (all of case 1 and 88 of case 0), and the shepherd launched `fanout.py 672` as "672:13 fanout" (`fanout_672_journal.jsonl`, `fanout_672.log`, `scratch/fanout672/run.err`). Its workers run at niceness 20 until relaunched, because the shepherd's launches added `nice -n 15` to the inherited 15; that is fixed for future launches. While it runs, do not edit focus.py's fingerprinted files (focus.py, focus480.py, focus672.py, focus480_certificates.json.gz, certbb.py, mstar.py, overnight_v5.py): the jobs and the splice depend on that code hash. Quals: `quals_fanout.py` (controls: (20, 5) excluded, (16, 4) witness), `quals_fanout_progress.py`; the progress section shows the run, with Lindy on the cases' LP work at the pool's rate. At 17:20 PDT fanout was relaunched by hand twice with two fixes, and its job files were kept. First, a job past its budget now finishes boxes more than 8 levels below its own, up to 4 budgets, and hands back at most 8: under the old rule 109 jobs had left 6,952 open. Second, SIGTERM to the driver now terminates its workers: before, they ran on as orphans. The shepherd adopts the hand-launched driver.

Process matching (Claude, 2026-10-23 18:11 PDT): at 17:16 the progress watcher and the shepherd both stopped on their one-process assertions. My own shell, whose command line contained "… python3 -B fanout.py 672 >> …", matched their patterns as a second fanout driver. Both now use `progress.runs(procs, script, *args)`: a live Python interpreter, with its options and then the script, by name or path, followed by those arguments. A shell that names the command is not matched, and neither is a zombie. survey_fanout also counts a job as running only if the latest driver started it; an earlier driver's unended jobs count as pending. Both monitors were restarted at 18:11.

Width-test benchmark (Claude, 2026-10-23, `scratch/width-bench/`): this tried certbb's width test, thin-direction cuts from `certbb.Guide`, inside focus.py-style depth-first search before each branch at depth at most D, and checked every token with `certbb.Check`. It did not speed up the search. On 72:7 and 120:7 it shrank trees by factors of 10 to 50, but each failed test costs about 100 to 200 LP solves, so wall time rose by 1.5 to 4 times. On 240:11 case 0, with 30,000 nodes and D up to 8, it found no cuts at all. That run took 56 s plain and 61 to 68 s with the test, with an identical closed fraction. The all-depths variant was stopped after 2.6 hours. The expected gain for 480:14 is parallelism: switch it to fanout once fanout 672 frees its slots.

672 speedup (Codex, 2026-10-24): `fanout.py` now restores the parent's LP basis before visiting its right child. Saved-box comparisons in `scratch/speed-fanout/` took 24.60 vs 11.68 s (case 0, 2,000 nodes), 104.08 vs 18.38 s (case 1, 2,000), and 105.31 vs 45.61 s (case 0, 8,000). The actual integrated worker took 45.11 s on that last box and produced the identical checked proof SHA. These bounded samples do not forecast completion time. Every exclusion still passes unchanged `certbb.Check`.

The same change reuses reflection: reversing positions p -> 671-p maps case 0 exactly to case 1. Only case 0 receives new LP jobs; `reflection.py` transforms its full proof (including the original focus prefix), and unchanged `focus.State` checks case 1 before any excluded verdict. Original job files and root indexes are retained. Interrupted proof splicing now replays and compares every previously committed splice record before resuming, including during reflection and after its terminal commit. Actual 480 preparation still has 195 cases and no eligible reflection pairs. The progress watcher reads the latest session's `reflections` field, excludes those targets from independent LP work estimates, and leaves them open until the final checked exclusion. No completion ETA is supplied while only proof assembly/replay remains.

480 investigation (Codex, 2026-10-24, `scratch/speed480/`): focus driver 7662 remains unchanged. Case 0 ended at 16,208,757 LPs; case 1 is current. On a saved case-1 depth-20 box, 8,000 nodes took 90.22 s with the existing warm starts and 28.38 s with restored parent bases; exactly excluded box volume was 73.342% versus 98.477%. A depth-1 comparison was mixed: 31.45 versus 26.53 s, but the restored variant closed less volume. These are bounded samples, not complete subtree comparisons or a completion forecast. A one-worker fanout migration would preserve the six-slot total but change scheduling to shallowest jobs first. Before such a migration, its progress accounting needs per-case prefix LP totals (assigning all prior LPs to case 1 would overstate its age), and session-specific worker counts for later expansion. No migration was performed.

The exact modulus-16 audit in `scratch/speed480/mod16.py` extends the existing forced-factor lattice: current case 1 has four possible patterns for its first eight mod-16 plus counts modulo 4; all sixteen counts are odd. Precisely 163,200 count vectors meet these lattice and capacity conditions, compared with 5,604,768,225 unrestricted lifts of the same mod-8 counts. An admissible vector was verified, so this does not exclude the case. Selective count branching is a candidate requiring benchmarks and compatible exact proof checking; full enumeration could be slower. The current case has no unused complement/reflection symmetry. Thirteen later representatives retain reversal symmetry (listed in `scratch/speed480/math-notes.txt`).

Branch audit for this pilot: the runner's explicit `if` statements verify returned witnesses, reuse cached preparation, choose resume versus initialization, prevent completed reruns, honor an already saved target witness, skip a recovered calibration, gate target work on calibration, stop attempts after a witness, and dispatch the Python entry point. Paired study completed at 17:01:29 PDT. Both controls recovered exactly verified witnesses; all seven baseline and seven wider target attempts returned empty, with no timeouts. Baseline totals: 21,766,057 nodes, 0.5919 enumeration seconds, 13.8242 attempt seconds. Wider totals: 153,546,063 nodes, 4.1478 enumeration seconds, 17.2245 attempt seconds. Thus widening explored 7.05 times as many nodes; enumeration cost also rose about sevenfold, but subprocess overhead dominated these short attempts, making total attempt time only 1.25 times as large. These are seven paired cases, not a full-sweep timing forecast or evidence of nonexistence. Every baseline target node count exactly reproduced its prior pilot result. Full study elapsed 37.54 seconds; focus480 PID7662 resumed automatically after 39.81 seconds. HTTP verified the final four-block graph, both controls, all fourteen empty target outcomes, and no ETA/exclusion claim; process inspection verified one watcher and no remaining study runner. Independent direct-power checks confirmed both controls. Prior search sources, journals and prepared inputs match their saved hashes. Details: `scratch/pruning288/result.json`. The wider curve is affordable on these cases and merits a broader placement sweep; the bound remains `9 <= m*(288) <= 10`. No broader sweep has started.

Broader 288 sweep launched (2026-10-24, 23:24 PDT): after approval to rotate 480's slot through calibrated witness searches, `sweep288.py` searches all 2,736 validated prepared cases with width 0.1 (fractions `0.1 + 0.9p`). One persistent worker amortizes imports and exact preparation; every attempt has a 30-second wall limit covering randomized reduction, recentering and enumeration. A timeout kills and reaps that worker before replacement; a crash or malformed response fails loudly. The fresh 240:10 control uses case 2, seed 20261026, and must recover before any target work. Target case i uses seed 20261024+i. The first seven completed target attempts must reproduce the prior wider study's exact outcomes, node counts and geometry hashes. Parent-side witness checks require integer signs and exact moments. Empty attempts and timeouts establish no exclusion. The append-only `sweep288_journal.jsonl` resumes completed attempts and refuses an unchanged completed rerun. An exclusive lock prevents duplicate drivers. The source hash is `b1495685d812482453c1d59384dab6a6e5aab49aeafad1b313b05a68443828ee`; do not edit its source files or prior search inputs while it runs. Exact sources are archived in `scratch/sweep288/sources/`; `verified.json` records the input hashes. All 25 preserved historical/proof files matched their pre-change hashes.

Launcher PID15846 uses `scratch/sweep288/lease.py`, a hash-checked adapter around the unchanged qualified earlier lease, to pause focus480 PID7662 in memory. Sweep driver PID15858 and its persistent worker run at nice15 with single-threaded BLAS. The lease terminates the entire sweep process group and resumes 480 on completion, exception or SIGTERM, with a 24-hour fail-safe ceiling. It checks five live fanout672 workers before borrowing the sixth slot; 672's driver PID76253 and its workers remain unchanged. Lease status/log: `scratch/sweep288/lease.json` and `lease.log`. 592:10 pilot preparation (Codex, 2026-10-25): `residue592.py` defines the archived 560:10 profile-zero control and a new symmetric 592:10 profile, without known witness coordinates. A bounded integer optimization selected seven fixed mod-27 orbits for 592, with specified mod-5 and mod-7 sums. Exact full moment/residue consistency and independent HNF column-lattice membership passed for both profiles: 560 has 145 free signs, 16 independent rows and dimension 129; 592 has 143 free signs, 16 rows and dimension 127. These linear checks do not establish a sign witness. The check used the qualified lease for 4.70 seconds and restored focus480 PID7662 to RN. `search592_geometry.py` shares one saturated kernel, lift system and reduced basis across a deterministic placement schedule. Its construction follows the archived `go560f.py`; exact row relations cover discarded constraints, rational projection radii and adaptive-precision recentering replace floating cancellation, and every candidate is checked exactly. The control uses profile zero, seed 6, slice 2/4; the target uses seed 20261025, slice 0/1. The archived control's first two witnesses fell within its first sixteen placements, but this is not a reproduced runtime forecast. Fixed pruning shape: (0.3, 1.5, 1). fpylll can report an unsigned-minus-one per-level node counter after immediate failure: the raw total and level counters are preserved, and `nodes` is null when that sentinel is present.

`search592.py` prepares 32 placements per phase and runs one persistent worker. Each preparation has a 600-second wall limit and each attempt a 30-second wall limit. The first exactly verified 560 recovery stops calibration and unlocks at most 32 target attempts. A target witness stops immediately; empty or timed-out attempts prove no exclusion. The append-only journal checks source/profile/library hashes, placement and basis identities, transitions, and exact witnesses on resume; unchanged completed batches are not restarted. A timeout kills and reaps the worker; its deterministic replacement must reproduce the saved plan and basis. The new lease adapter uses the unchanged earlier cleanup core, with a one-hour ceiling and automatic 480 resumption. Do not launch without green runner/geometry/residue/progress/lease quals. Sources remain separate from the earlier 288 experiments and live proof sources.

Branch audit for 592: fifteen explicit runner branches validate timeout metadata and witnesses, distinguish preparation from a phase identity, enforce the receive deadline, start a missing worker, skip closing an absent worker, select resume versus initialization, refuse completed reruns, honor an already saved target witness, skip a recovered control, enforce the target gate, stop on a new target witness, stop calibration on recovery, and dispatch the script and worker entry points. Geometry predicates select independent/zero rows, free and fixed coordinates, short particular solutions and kernel/lift rows. This target pilot is narrow: all 32 placements share one prefix (the first six variable fixed-orbit choices), varying only the last orbit. Their exact squared in-span radii range from 36.79 to 47.32, versus 119.71 to 120.62 for the five control placements. Native certificate experiment (Codex, 2026-10-25, `scratch/native672/`): a bounded replay of the saved 672 case-0 depth-8 box instruments native HiGHS calls separately from producer arithmetic, independent checker, row construction, bookkeeping and JSON/gzip. The 2,000-step plain and measured runs reproduce proof SHA `2ddfce7462c458a75e9d15a277e2b333f7a9fdf0`, 278,549 simplex iterations and the same 35-box frontier. Exclusive measured wall time sums exactly to 9.68865 seconds: HiGHS.run 63.88%, producer lower_bound 11.03%, checker.feed 11.46%, row rebuilding 2.25%. Timings include cold per-trial LP setup and benchmark wrappers; production reuses its LP across jobs, so these samples are not completion forecasts. `profile-case0.json` contains the full accounting and 32 exact bound calls. The initial loader failed because the archived preparation contains (fingerprint, equations, cases); a new real-input qual now enforces that exact format and current fingerprint. The failed lease restored 480 after 0.82 seconds, and its log is retained.

`native_bound.cpp` is an isolated C++/GMP implementation of the same sparse arbitrary-integer bound. It owns copied coefficients, imports/exports Python integers through signed-magnitude binary conversion, and rejects malformed inputs. The immutable-row adapter's identity guard avoids stale cached coefficients. Only the producer's private function namespace uses it: the original `certbb` module and every `certbb.Check` call stay unchanged. Ten arithmetic quals cover brute-force boxes, duplicate positions, zero/empty cases, cancellation, strict input rejection, 64-bit transitions, ownership and signed 20,001-bit values. Two compiled integration quals require identical exact witness/exclusion controls and reject a changed proof. Seven profiler quals cover timer accounting, unchanged proofs/frontiers, a forged producer rejected by the original checker, cached rows and the real preparation format. Eight launcher quals cover preflight, exclusive records/lock and restoration on failure. Source snapshots and hashes are in `sources/` and `verified.json`; compiler/Python/GMP/binary metadata are in `environment.json`.

The first native build exposed missing default C++ standard-library headers. `build_native.py` now uses the installed SDK and verified C++ header directory explicitly; no installation or global setting changed. Build2 succeeded in 1.51 seconds and restored 480. The remaining compiler warnings are in installed GMP literal declarations and the zero-initialized unused PyModuleDef fields. The build has a 120-second compiler wall/CPU limit. The prototype is Python 3.13/macOS-specific and has not been integrated into live searches.

`compare.py` compares rebuilt-row Python, cached-row Python, and cached-row native producers in ABCCBA order, two samples of each per box, keeping every Python certificate check. It tests the prior depth-8 box and a recent completed depth-9 box (`recent-job.meta.json` records its source hash), 2,000 LP steps each. Every variant must match statuses, iteration count, proof/token/frontier hashes and serialized sizes. The comparison lease is running; 480 is paused only for this bounded phase while all five 672 workers continue. The unchanged qualified lease core provides a 20-minute ceiling and always attempts identity-checked restoration. `compare-lease.json` and `compare.log` record the actual outcome; do not infer completion from this preparation note.

Branch audit: bench.py has six explicit branches for its 32-call capture limit, parent-basis restoration, witness termination, branch children, complete-exclusion finish and entry point; tuple selections choose measured/plain and cached/rebuilt modes. compare.py has only its entry-point branch plus filters for grouping/report fields. The native C++ source has ten explicit branches: failed preconditions, failed C-API results, small-integer conversion, two binary-conversion failures, input sign, small-result conversion, output sign, and two C-API argument parsers. Its one ternary selects the smaller exact endpoint product. C++ exception handlers contain failures at the Python boundary. The Python adapter has no explicit branches; the build and launcher each have only their entry-point branch. Launcher exception paths distinguish timeout from failure; process cleanup is inherited unchanged. Source files used by live proof searches were not edited. Git command restrictions are present in the inspected Claude settings; that observation does not establish enforcement by Codex's separate runtime.

Native comparison stopped at its bounded limit on 2026-10-25 at 11:00 PDT. The depth-8 box completed all six ABCCBA repeats, with median wall times 9.61275 s (rebuilt-row Python), 9.31929 s (cached-row Python), and 8.39156 s (cached-row native): 12.70% less total time, comprising a 3.05% cache gain and a further 9.95% native gain relative to cached Python. Each trial checked every proof token with the unchanged Python checker; the runner asserted identical LP statuses, iterations, proof/token/frontier hashes and serialized sizes before logging this box. Its logged proof SHA matches the independently preserved profile. Both repeats' wall/CPU times remain in `compare.log`, but the complete per-trial proof/frontier records were held for final output and did not survive the later timeout. The recent depth-9 box did not finish its six-repeat comparison; neither its partial timing nor a second speedup estimate is available. No `comparison.json` was written. The fresh LP/basis can follow a much harder tree than the original production job; the cause of the delay was not profiled, so no solver-specific diagnosis is established.

Preserve this experiment before any revision; a future runner should persist each trial immediately so a later timeout cannot discard its detailed records.

672 LP investigation (Codex, 2026-10-25, `scratch/lp672/`): engineering effort stayed on 672 while retaining five production workers and the original 480 process. The qualified native experiment lease temporarily borrowed 480's slot for each diagnostic; `scratch/native672/lp672_entry.py` adds a 180-second OS alarm that also interrupts a blocked native solver call. A watchdog stop appears in the outer lease as returncode -14 and status `failed`, with automatic restoration, not as a completed replay. Every solver call is durably logged before and after execution; the original integer checker accepts every exclusion. The 53 protected source/input files in `before.json` remained byte-identical. No production code or mathematical bound changed.

The latest-session corpus had 113 completed 80,000-LP jobs; its slowest 20 accounted for 80.5% of recorded job wall time. Depth-9 jobs ranged from 3.59 to 253 ms per LP. Those journal records do not separate CPU, sleep, numerical iterations or scheduling. A one-second sample of one live worker was entirely inside HiGHS; it is not a whole-job profile. The recent saved box's cold replay checked 862 steps before the three-minute alarm: 309 calls reached 20,000 iterations, and completed HiGHS calls consumed 176.50 seconds. Production retains solver state between jobs, whereas these replays start fresh, so the measurements do not forecast production completion.

An unrestricted-case basis seed let that recent replay finish 2,000 steps in 15.07 seconds and exclude 7.96479 times the baseline's binary-box measure. It was rejected as a general change: the preselected cheap depth-9 box instead rose from 15.59 to 119.09 seconds for only 4.16% more excluded measure. Preserve both results; do not select only the favorable seed run. The preselected median and expensive boxes were not subsequently used for long comparisons after this regression.

`stall.py` captured the first capped LP after 263 checked steps, with 126 fixed variables. All 34 exact row intervals remain individually feasible. Fresh ABBA dual/primal comparisons reproduced the dual cap twice; primal produced independently checked exclusions in nine iterations from the pre-cap basis and 148 from the post-cap basis. Saved basis statuses omit factorization and other internal solver state. The subsequent same-instance retry therefore received its own search replay. A fixed 200-iteration primal retry after a dual cap reached 860 checked steps at the alarm, with 310 retries and 305 still capped. A single predeclared 2,000-iteration variant reached 1,651 steps, with 343 retries and 109 still capped. Both excluded essentially the same binary-box measure as the baseline (about 3.718021446e-9 of the saved job). The larger variant's additional excluded measure was only about 1.62e-27 of that job. More processed nodes did not establish a useful proof-progress gain, and neither retry was deployed.

The retry's only new search branch is the exact iteration-limit trigger; it makes one primal call on unchanged bounds and restores both original options in `finally`. Diagnostic entry points, trace selectors, strict event validation, and qual-only branches are inventoried individually in `branches.json`. New source snapshots and hashes are in `sources/` and `verified.json`. Mock quals cover tracing, watchdog dispatch, capture provenance, solver comparisons, retry restoration/failure, and physical-call accounting; actual 16:4 witness and 20:5 exclusion controls passed, and forged certificates were rejected by the original checker. Frozen benchmark summary counters count the final call per search step; `retry_report.py` and `retry2000_report.py` separately count all physical calls, including the initial capped dual work. Timeout totals remain censored. `result.json`, `final-audit.json`, and `restoration.json` record the complete outcome. Final process inspection found focus480 PID7662 running, the same five 672 workers under driver PID76253, one watcher PID53334, no diagnostic children, niceness 15, and 167.58 GiB free. Preserve these experiments before any revision. No further option sweep or live restart was performed.

`fanout_lease.py` owns the unbounded production fanout child in a separate process group. 720/816 gap audit (Codex, 2026-10-25, `scratch/gaps720816/`): both bounds remain `10 <= m*(n) <= 14`. The 24 saved exclusions among 816's 28 prime-residue combinations were independently replayed with the unchanged integer certificate checker, without LP solves. Four combinations remain, or two classes under complement/reflection. All eight saved 720 combinations survive their earlier relaxation. No fresh production search was launched and the eight-worker 672 allocation was unchanged.

The new exact quotient-parity condition is `D_M in P + 2 L_P(M)`, where `P=(x-1)^14(x+1)`, `M` divides 16, and `L_P(M)` is the integer lattice generated by cyclic shifts of P. Both lengths are 16 times an odd integer. Comparing coefficient parity with `P*(1+x^16+...+x^(n-16))` proves the affine condition; it is stronger than the old homogeneous residue lattice plus coordinate parity. Combined with capacities and 816's necessary modulo-5 counts, only two modulo-4 signed profiles remain: `(-64,64,64,-64)` and its negative. Proofs are written out in `mod4.py` and `affine.py`; their necessary conditions do not establish sign witnesses or exclusions by themselves.

The first capped modulo-8/modulo-5 screen rejected 304 vectors before its 1,000-node cap. That prefix was misleading and is preserved as `joint8_audit.json`, not treated as an exclusion. An independent complete pair-table enumeration in `joint8.py` examined 5,041 pairs per half for each modulo-4 profile and found 3,281 affine vectors per profile. All 32 modulo-5 transport inequalities leave 201, 288, 288, and 201 vectors in the four profile combinations. `joint8_complete.json` retains every vector and original-generator certificate; `joint8_transport.json` records the transport audit. A separate standard-library polynomial-inverse calculation checked one survivor, its generator certificate and all 32 inequalities (`survivor_independent.json`). These conditions therefore do not prove `m*(816) <= 13`. The stricter modulo-16 screen remains capped and unresolved.

All 26 new quals passed, including an actual known 72:6 sign witness through the complete modulo-8 enumeration, an actual 12:2 sign control, small exhaustive comparisons, forged-certificate rejection and strict cap behavior. The affine helper's original 1,000-node limit and quals are preserved. The standalone standard-library checker `joint8_transport.py` independently reconstructs every saved original-generator certificate and checks all 32 transport inequalities; its reproduction is saved separately as `joint8_transport_recheck.json`, preserving the earlier artifacts. Branch inventory: `mod4.py` has no explicit if statement; `affine.py` has five for budget, capacity, terminal row, pivot choice and entry point; `joint8.py` has its entry-point guard and one conditional expression constructing the constant versus variable columns. The transport checker has its entry-point guard and a comprehension predicate retaining records that pass all 32 inequalities. No fingerprinted proof/search source was edited.

For a future 720:11 witness experiment, fixing the known 384:10 prefix and solving for a symmetric 336:10 suffix gives a 156-dimensional system after the contemplated residue rows, compared with 180 in the failed reverse direction. The combined residue/moment integer-coset feasibility still needs verification; smaller dimension is not a speed or success estimate. Known products and audited two-block joins did not immediately improve either lower bound. The more concrete next exclusion experiment is 816:14 with the new affine restrictions and the remaining exact moment constraints; it has not been scheduled.

Review of Astra's work (Claude, 2026-10-25, `scratch/ideas-20261025/`): three workflows (understand, ideate, verify) produced the briefs `understand.txt`, `ideas.txt`, `critic.txt` and `verify.txt`, with each agent's code and outputs under `ideate/` and `verify/`. Every witness below was checked in exact integers by at least three separate scripts. None of these results is recorded in `mstar.py`, `mstar.js` or `index.html` yet, because `mstar.py` is in focus.fingerprint and an edit would make the 672/480 continuations unresumable.
- Witnesses: 592:10 (symmetric, `verify/w-592/witness_592.jsonl`), so m*(592) = 10; and 880, 928, 944, 976 and 992 at order 11 (antisymmetric, the go800f recipe with first-solution enumeration; `ideate/witnesses/witnesses_880.jsonl`, `verify/w-order11/witnesses_*.jsonl`), so m* = 11 at each.
- 752:11 is impossible, so 9 <= m*(752) <= 10. Mod 3 forces plus counts (4, 247, 125) or the complement. Relaxing class 2 to integers leaves a congruence on the 4+4 exceptional signs in a group of order 3^45. A meet in the middle over all C(251,4)^2 placements finds 0 survivors (`ideate/exclusions/mitm752.py`). An independent reimplementation with Smith-coordinate keys (`verify/v752-impl/`) reproduces every bucket total and finds 0 for both profiles. Its controls: 384:11 gives 0 of 23,716, 496:10 gives 0, and both a full-size search for the 800:11 witness's own 4+4 and a planted 752 placement are recovered. A line-by-line referee (`verify/v752-referee/`) found no error. There is no compact certificate: a recheck reruns the enumeration (about 1 CPU-minute).
- 288:11 is impossible by 16 exact root certificates (`ideate/exclusions/cert288.json`, stdlib checker `verify/v288/`). Each also reduces to a single-cell counting cut on the mod-35 table. hi(288) = 10 then no longer rests on the BGPW citation.
- 672:13: random-path probes match the production code node for node on complete trees. Production's own frontier gives mean leaf depth 42.99 +- 0.47, a floor of at least 1e13 LPs at 95% (about 1,000 years at 313 LP/s). 480:14 is likely of the same order, by extrapolation (`verify/v-floors/`). Astra's parent-basis restoration deepens these trees: 672 E[D] is about 43 with it and about 37 without.
- HiGHS 1.12.0 (SciPy 1.17.1) sets the zero-cost fallback `max_abs_cost = 1` only inside `if (report_cost_perturbation)`, which equals `output_flag`. With output off and a zero objective, the dual cost perturbation is therefore exactly 0, the cause of the 20,000-iteration stalls. Upstream PR #3226 fixed this on 2026-08-22, unreleased. Setting `output_flag` True with `log_to_console` False restores the perturbation: 1.55x less CPU over 8 complete subtrees, and a stalled box closed in 11 LPs (`verify/v-highs/`). Caps from near-optimal warm starts remain.
- 432:12 reduces exactly to cases A and B with f'(-1) = +8192, by a 2-adic saturation lemma: h(-1) is odd when F's (x+1)-multiplicity mod 2 is 2^v - 1. The lemma is proved on paper and checked exhaustively on 7.5M small cases. The remaining tree is 1e9-3e10 LPs, uncertain by at least 20x (`verify/v432/`). 816:14 reduces to 16 subcases (8 orbits) whose sizes are unknown: the smallest did not finish in 57,506 LPs. 864:14's subcases have E[D] 36-54 and are out of reach (`verify/v816/`).
- Root-reduction files that hold verdict strings instead of certificates (`ideate/theory/deriv_split_*.json`) cannot be rechecked; `verify/v432/` and `verify/v816/` regenerated the certificates. `ideate/literature/probes/recheck288.py`'s divisibility filter is unsound when p-1 divides m. All probes ran at nice 19, which does not protect nice-15 workers. The production processes and tracked files were left unchanged.

672:13 and 480:14 stopped (Claude, 2026-10-25 18:55 PDT; dreeves: "yes to stopping 672:13 etc."). The steps:
- SIGTERM to guardian 94817. Its cleanup ended fanout driver 94848 and its 8 workers, resumed focus 480 (PID 7662), and wrote the paired `resume` record to `focus_480_pauses.jsonl`.
- 480 then ran for about 15 s and got SIGTERM.
Neither journal ends in a finish, so the shepherd (still PID 94847) treats both as stopped and never relaunches them. The watcher (94849) keeps writing progress.js. Resuming either run would need the archived proof sources, because the fingerprint covers mstar.py, which recording the new results changes.

Run folds (Claude, 2026-10-25, at dreeves's request): each run's fold in "Search progress" opens or stays closed as the browser last left it. It is stored in localStorage as "progress-fold <program> <n> <order>" = "1" while open, the same mechanism as the section's own "progress-open". A fold that was never clicked starts closed, whatever the run's state. The summary names the run's state: running, paused, frozen, completed or aborted. An ended run counts as completed when its journal recorded an outcome, and aborted otherwise. progress.js now carries `outcome` for every run. Quals: quals_index q_progress_folds_stay_as_left, q_progress_folds_are_remembered_across_loads, q_progress_summary_names_each_run_s_state; quals_progress q_js_is_one_progress_data_call.

Fingerprints after the residues split (Claude, 2026-10-26; dreeves: "if that's what you recommend, sure"): mstar.py's residue enumeration is now residues.py. focus.fingerprint hashes focus.py, focus432.py, focus480.py, focus672.py, focus432_certificates.json.gz, focus480_certificates.json.gz, certbb.py, residues.py and overnight_v5.py; fanout_code hashes fanout.py and reflection.py. A byte changed in any of them, comments included, stops the next fanout session at its start, so they stay frozen while a fanout run such as 432:12's is going and until reprove.py has rechecked its finish. mstar.py is in neither, and no proof path imports it: mstar.py's tables may change during a run, and new witnesses go into them, not into overnight_v5.EXAMPLES. width.py, which certbb.py imports, is in no fingerprint either; in the prelaunch review's call traces of the 432 prefix, a 432 fanout session and a reprove run, only its module body ran. The 2026-10-23 fanout note's list, with mstar.py, was 672's. Before the 432:12 launch, `fanout.load` came to refuse a job file that hands back a box not strictly within its own (a forged cycle had made a session's start walk forever), and reprove.py's totals came to name code, fanout_code and reprove_code (the sha256 of reprove.py's bytes), refusing if any of them changed during the recheck. The launch checklist, with the final values, is `scratch/record-20261025/launch432/wire/proposal.md`.

mstar.py, index.html, progress.py, shepherd.py and the quals are free. Stop the driver only with SIGTERM; the shepherd never relaunches a stopped run. Relaunch by hand with proposal.md's step-7 command. Record a(11) only after reprove.
The page's ETA starts at minutes and grows: 432's probe floors (about 5e5 LP solves in all) are far below the expected 1e9-3e10, so Gott's rule on the work done dominates for now.

432:12 switched to a random job order (Claude, 2026-10-26 22:20 PDT; dreeves: "ok, good, yeah"). Since then:
- **Job order:** fanout.py draws each job uniformly at random from the pool of boxes not done, with random.Random(seed). The seed is the session's ordinal and is in its session record.
- **Probes:** each records every dive's depth (depths).
- **The switch record:** written by `fanout.py switch N` under the work directory's lock, so only while no session runs. It lets a run continue under changed fanout code: a session's and assembly's code must be the code in force, the header's fanout_code or the last switch's to.
- **The 432 run:** its journal switched from 79f1038e…309f to 07d16e94…7a6d at 22:19:47. Session 2 (driver PID 33227, seed 2) has run since 22:19:57, with session 1's ~2,900 job files kept. The shepherd adopted the driver.
- **The watcher:** restarted first, as PID 31885, because the old process held a progress.py that knows neither switch records nor probe depths.
- **Frozen again:** fanout.py and reflection.py, at the new code. An unrecorded edit still stops the next session; a deliberate one needs the driver stopped with SIGTERM, `fanout.py switch 432`, then the step-7 relaunch.
- **Quals:**
  - quals_fanout: an independent replay of the draws; the one-worker sequences re-recorded, since the order changes LP states and so some trees;
  - quals_fanout_tree: five switch quals, with the 20:5 exclusion and 16:4 witness controls;
  - quals_fanout_progress: two;
  - quals_reprove: its forged journal now has a header.
- **Probe depths:** 300 dives per case never went deeper than 34 levels, while the pool holds boxes 139 levels deep. So Knuth's estimate from them (about 2.5e8 nodes) is unreliable.

Pool estimate for fanout runs (Claude, 2026-10-26 22:53 PDT; dreeves: "sure", to building it). progress.py now estimates fanout's ETA from the pool's random draws, replacing Gott's rule on the cases' work.
- **The model:** a branching recursion by depth over the sampled jobs, in 4-level bands. A band with no sample borrows its nearest band's, the deeper on a tie.
- **The total and band:** summed over the latest session's pool, with a 200-replicate bootstrap within bands. The probes' leasts are a floor.
- **No estimate** without a pool record and a sample, so old-format journals get none.
- **New journal fields:** fanout.py journals a pool record after each session's walk, and open_depths on each job_end.
- **432's second switch:** from 07d16e94… to f29b4e7d…b41b at 22:49:32. Session 3 (driver PID 68450, seed 3) has run since 22:49:42. Watcher PID 67917.
- **The estimate's limits:**
  - Session 2's 226 draws didn't journal open_depths, so the estimate starts from session 3's.
  - A preview from session 2's draws, with depths read from its job files (scratch/random432/preview_eta.py), gave a median of 111 days, 90% band 35 to 331.
  - The bootstrap band covers sampling within bands, not borrowing between them, so early estimates are too narrow.
- **Quals:**
  - quals_fanout_progress: eight fanout ETA quals rewritten from Gott's rule to the pool estimate, five added;
  - quals_allocation_progress: two rewritten (rates through pool_estimate);
  - quals_fanout: one added (the pool record and open_depths), one updated (the pool record precedes the probes).

Table and chart to 1024 (Claude, 2026-10-27; dreeves, relaying Stan Wagon: the upper part "should include 256, 512, and 1024 for completeness", "could the table and the chart go up to 1024 as the natural stopping point, instead of 1000?"; dreeves: "how about making our color-coded table totally subsume the BGPW table?", then "multiples of 16 with one-sentence explanation sounds good").
- **The table:** the new results' upper rows now hold every multiple of 8 up to 304, as Table 2 does, then every multiple of 16 up to 1024 (`mstar.UPPER`), in six rows starting at 8, 136, 240, 480, 672 and 848; the three rows of powers of 2 are unchanged. Table 2's unchanged entries are copied uncolored, titled "The published paper (2021)"; 288's "9 or 10" is written 9–10. 512 and 1024 appear in both parts, as the same cell. Table 2 itself stays above. Built by `scratch/upto1024/merge.py` and `apply.py`, which asserted dreeves's lines kept.
- **Two frames** (dreeves, later that day: "can we split the table into the non-powers-of-2 and the powers-of-2 and move the note about multiples of 8 or 16 under the non-power-of-2 table?"): the new-results block has one `div.scroll` for the six upper rows and one for the three rows of powers of 2. `quals_index.new_frames` reads both; quals that read only the first frame would miss the powers.
- **The sentence:** Claude's, between the two frames, with a TODO recap: odd multiples of 8 are capped at 7 by BGPW's Theorem 4.1 and all equal 7 from 200 on, so past 304 only multiples of 16 are listed. `quals_index.q_new_results_say_which_lengths` checks its numbers (8, 304, 16, 7, 200) against the rows and mstar.js, not its wording.
- **288's note** moved from under Table 2 to the remarks (dreeves: "just clarify that that one isn't technically new but is rederived here"): an entry between 240's and 304's, in an `<li>` without a class and with an empty marker, as its uncolored cell; q_288_bound_is_proved_here and q_new_results_state_what_the_chart_proves hold each remark marked like its cell.
- **The block label** now reads "Extended results since the paper", dreeves's edit.
- **The chart:** `mstar.NMAX` is 1024. The new columns are settled by the two lemma and joins except 1008, 10 to 13: lo by the join 240 + 768, hi by a new residue claim, order 14 mod 5 (`scratch/upto1024/residue1008.py`). Order 13 at 1008 stays open: (5, 25), (7, 49) and (4, 8, 16, 32) capped at 10^7 nodes in both sweeps (`order13.py`). 1008 has a new-results cell and a remark in the form of 720's; no changelog statement, since its lower bound is built. 1024 is now a column: pow2.js's 2^10 hiWhy became `["mstar", ["norm", 3]]`, the page's powers-of-2 script has `NMAX = 1024` and an empty `LONG`, and the caption's p_1024 clause is gone (its wording before 51c0de3 restored).
- **mstar.page_sources** no longer pins a table count: it asserts the structure instead (UPPER, k rising from 3, copies equal to Table 2, every standing Table 2 entry copied, duplicates identical). The page has twelve tables of class block.
- **Quals changed with the spec:** quals_mstar (nmax 1024, 74 NEW entries, merged-table strictness cases via `edit_cells`, 1024's column, a new qual for 1002 to 1024, 1008 gray); quals_index (NMAX, nine new-results rows, counts, duplicates in `new_cells` and the remarks qual, two new quals); quals_pow2 (`2^k <= NMAX` for "mstar", no long examples, `hi_of(11, ["mstar", ...])` as the broken case); quals_pow2_page (LONG empty, caption names no p_N outside LONG, "mstar above 10", the "joins long block" violation dropped, the driver's column count from mstar.js).
- **preview.png:** zoom 0.44 fits Table 2, the two tables, the sentence and the legend. Chrome wrote the shot and then exited 2 in its teardown, so the shot was taken by hand with the builder's flags.

Symmetry Conjecture test at 528 (Claude, 2026-10-27; dreeves: "sure, let's try lattice search for 528 ... you can pause the 432:12 search"). BGPW's conjecture (§6: a nonempty P(n, m) holds a (−1)^m symmetric LP) predicted a symmetric order-10 LP of length 528, the one even n ≤ 1024 whose best known order had only a non-symmetric example (SClaude[10][528], a matched join). `scratch/sym528/` holds a copy of the w688 pipeline (its four quals pass there), w10a's derive.py, and `residue_screen.py` and `expect.py`. Every modulus up to 49 admits a symmetric order-10 residue profile at 528 (and at 288). Designs d528_K7F105p* (d 121, 2^17.6 expected per placement) and d528_K8F105p* (d 103) screened at about 2^33.8 and 2^30.3 model nodes per expected witness. With 432:12 paused 294 s by `scratch/wish2/pause432.py`, the 560 control came back at CVP 29 as archived, and nine runs found four distinct symmetric 528:10 witnesses within a minute (`scratch/sym528/witnesses_528.jsonl`), each checked by check.py, poly.py and mstar.order: exact order 10, mirror-symmetric. The bounds stay 10 ≤ m*(528) ≤ 12. Correction, the same day: 528 was not an open test of the conjecture after all, because BGPW already had an unpublished order-10 example there that settles the point; the four new ones are only further examples. One of them is on the page as p528 in 528's entry (mstar.PAGE_CODES; column 528 now cites it). 288's prediction (m*(288) = 9) stays untested: symmetric order 10 there is excluded by BGPW's ILP, so a counterexample is non-symmetric, and a full-space lattice has dimension about 278.

288:10 exhaustive search (Claude, 2026-10-27 16:23 PDT; dreeves: "pause 432:12 to run 288 as hard as we can first"). Table 2's last open entry; BGPW's Symmetry Conjecture says m*(288) = 9, and since their ILP excludes symmetric order 10 there, a witness would refute it (or their ILP).
- **Code:** `scratch/x288/overnight_v5.py` is a copy of the frozen root file plus two data lines: SETTINGS[(288, 10)] = cascade (3, 5, 7, 4, 8, 9), budgets 10000 × 6 and 2000000 at the bottom (384@11's), and EXAMPLES[(288, 9)], the antisymmetrization of the paper's 144:8 example, for the startup control (passed 16:25). CODE is the original's. Journal and log: `scratch/x288/overnight_v5_288_10_journal.jsonl` and `.log`; it resumes from the journal.
- **Lease:** `scratch/x288/run288.py` (wrapper PID in `scratch/x288/run288.out`) paused 432:12 with `scratch/wish2/pause432.py` and stopped its two-hour watchdog, so 432:12 stays paused until the search ends; it resumes 432:12 when the child exits, however it exits, or on SIGTERM to the wrapper (which first ends the search's process group). A SIGKILL skips that: then run `pause432.py resume` by hand. Quals: `scratch/x288/quals_run288.py` (3).
- **On the progress page** since 17:19 (dreeves: "why don't i see it in the search progress section?"): progress.RUNS has (288, 10), and progress.DIRS maps it to scratch/x288. files() loads that directory's overnight_v5.py once, asserts its CODE equals the root module's, and takes the run's SETTINGS from it. Qual: quals_progress q_files_reads_a_run_from_its_own_directory (67/67 pass). The watcher was restarted as PID 90203 (search592_progress.py --watch, PYTHONPATH as before, log scratch/allocate672/progress.log). Its first ETA, from 0.9 h of data, was 40-193 days.
- **Running dots** (dreeves: "why are there zero orange (running) cells?", then "is there a cheaper way to have the page not lie?"): overnight_v5's log names a node only when it is done, and in a first pass the watcher splits nothing, so the replay's running nodes had no keys and the sea showed none. sea() now takes the parent's key of each unnamed running node and draws it as a running dot in that parent's group (no extra splits); groups stay in tree order. Qual: q_sea_places_running_nodes_without_keys (68/68 pass). Watcher restarted as PID 15453. The replay counts 10 running where a log line says 9: lines are written as a node finishes, and search() then refills to 10.
- **Queued dots** (dreeves: "i don't understand how the sea of dots looks like everything's mostly settled but the ETA is like 100 days", then "what's the objectively correct thing to do?"): the sea drew only nodes with keys, so the ~3,100 queued cases (the replay's stack, unnamed) were missing and the settled dots looked like most of the search. sea()'s `unnamed` is now (parent key, status) pairs: each unnamed running node, and each unnamed node on the stack as pending, drawn in its parent's group. Every case that exists is now a dot; how big each unsplit case is stays the ETA's job. Qual renamed q_sea_places_nodes_without_keys (68/68 pass; quals_fanout_progress and quals_allocation_progress OK). Watcher restarted as PID 39188; at 18:11 the sea read settled 328, open 7, pending 3,086, running 10 (screenshot: `scratch/x288/shot_sea.py` → `scratch/x288/sea.png`).
- **Open leaves:** by 17:40, 4 bottom cases had come back UNKNOWN at the 2,000,000-LP budget (about an hour each). The pass will end NOT FINISHED with such leaves, as 240@11's did (606), unless they are handed to boxsplit.py or a bigger budget.

528 set (same day; dreeves: "sure to the additional 528 bipartition"): the sets section has `SClaudeSym[10][528]`, p528's +1 positions, right after the join's `SClaude[10][528]`. mstar.PAGE_SET_KEYS is now (name, m, n); page_sources parses only the sets section and asserts every set's name is SClaude or SClaudeSym.

boxsplit trial on 288:10 (Claude, 2026-10-27 20:40 PDT; Astra recommended it, dreeves: "ok"). `scratch/x288/trial/trial288.py` runs boxsplit.close_tree (by 11, then 13, budgets 10000 and 2000000, as 304@9's) on bottom cases of the running search, each in its own worker process beside it at nice 15, in two arms at once: base (certbb as is) and a2 (HiGHS output flag on, log off, as focus.case_lp). Limits per case-arm: 3,600 CPU s (RLIMIT_CPU), 2 GiB resident (swap was 26.7 of 27.6 GB used), 3 h wall. It reads the search's journal and log and writes only under `scratch/x288/trial/`. Quals: `quals_trial288.py` (12, all green; controls: 304@9's TRUTH tally, a 5 s CPU cap, SIGTERM cleanup). quals_boxsplit was 36/36 first.
- **Plan** (`runs/plan.json`): a 606-line snapshot of the journal; the 4 capped leaves (UNKNOWN at 2M) first in sha1 order and 4 bottom cases closed after more than 100,000 LP solves, spread by LP solves. boxsplit.control() for 288:10 passed in both arms.
- **Results** (`runs/results.jsonl`; `trial288.py report scratch/x288/trial/runs`): all 8 closed in both arms by the split by 11 alone, no node open, at most 140 MB per worker, the whole trial in under 5 minutes. Base CPU s against the search's solve seconds (wall, 10 workers on 10 cores): capped 20, 34, 14, 30 against 3,848, 4,093, 3,718 and 3,792 s without closing; late 31, 8, 9, 8 against 3,019, 518, 268 and 181 s (1,624,609 to 104,607 LP solves). a2 used 10-15% less CPU on the larger cases, with the same LP solves; the same on the small ones.
- **What it implies** (not yet acted on): with a bottom budget of 10,000, 29% of the 546 bottom cases in the snapshot would have gone to boxsplit and 97% of the 71.3M LP solves would not have been spent; at the trial's mean of 19 CPU s per case, about 2 h of work instead of about 37 worker-hours. That rests on 8 cases.

288:10 bottom budget 10,000 and the boxsplit chain (Claude, 2026-10-27 21:44-22:05 PDT; dreeves: "ok" to the trial's proposal).
- **Budget:** `scratch/x288/overnight_v5.py` SETTINGS[(288, 10)] is now (10000,) * 7. SETTINGS is outside CODE, so the journal's header still matches; records UNKNOWN at 2,000,000 are reused as open leaves. quals_progress's q_files_reads_a_run_from_its_own_directory pins the new value.
- **The chain:** `scratch/x288/run288.py` (wrapper PID in run288.out; relaunched twice, 21:44 and 21:51, 432:12 running for 18 s and 1 s between) now runs STEPS under the one lease: overnight_v5.py 288 10, then `boxsplit.py 288 10`, then `handoff288.py`, then overnight_v5.py 288 10 again, each only if the one before exited 0 (`/bin/sh -c 'a && b && c && d'`, in scratch/x288 so its copies are imported). overnight_v5 exits 0 after logging a witness, so the steps after the first pass would still run then; boxsplit exits 1 on a witness. 432:12 resumes when the chain ends, however it ends. Quals: quals_run288.py (5).
- **boxsplit copy:** `scratch/x288/boxsplit.py` is the repo's boxsplit.py plus EXTENSION[(288, 10)] = ((11, 13), (10000, 2000000)) and its comment; its CODE is the original's; its journal and log are `scratch/x288/overnight_v5_288_10_boxsplit_*`. The trial's harness now imports this copy too (scratch/x288 is first on its path).
- **Handoff:** `scratch/x288/handoff288.py` is scratch/boxsplit-240/handoff.py's steps as functions: refuses while overnight_v5.py or boxsplit.py 288 10 runs (progress.runs), without the driver's journal, for a CLOSED leaf that is not open in the search's journal or has no INFEASIBLE pool under it, and when its before-copy (`overnight_v5_288_10_journal.before_handoff.jsonl`) exists. Quals: `scratch/x288/quals_boxsplit288.py` (5, with the copy's diff and its 288 control).
- **Progress page:** progress.EXTENDED = ((288, 10),), shown once the driver's log exists; box_files and survey_box find a run's files through files() (DIRS), so the root boxsplit.py needs no 288 extension. Three new quals; quals_progress 71/71, fanout and allocation progress quals OK. Watcher restarted as PID 85449.
- **First numbers under 10,000** (22:05): 297 bottom cases closed and 371 UNKNOWN (56%, against 29% in the earlier sample), each UNKNOWN about 23 s. The page shows no ETA for the new pass until a subtree finishes in it.

Bottom-budget measurement on 288:10 (Claude, 2026-10-27 22:40 PDT; dreeves: "ok" to measuring whether a lower budget would pay). `scratch/x288/trial/plan10k.py` plans trial288's harness on a 2,702-line snapshot: 6 cases UNKNOWN at 10,000 (sha1 order) and 6 closed after 1,007 to 9,791 LP solves (spread by LP solves), and `plan10k.py costs` models each budget over the 2,020 bottom cases solved under 10,000 so far. A first attempt (`runs10k-stopped`) was stopped: trial288.plain_seconds took a node's last log line, which for a node solved before the 21:51 restart is the reuse line at 0.0 s; it now takes the most a node's lines say (q_plain_seconds_takes_the_solve_not_the_reuse; quals_trial288 15/15).
- **Results** (`runs10k`): all 12 closed in both arms. boxsplit's mean CPU s (close_tree's own, base arm): 17.4 on open cases (7 to 41), 3.5 on mid ones; plain search 2.16 ms per LP solve. Modeled seconds per bottom case, plain and boxsplit together: 12.7 at a budget of 1,000, 14.1 at 2,000, 15.5 at 3,000, 18.2 at 5,000, 24.7 at 10,000. a2 again 0-15% less CPU.
- **Scale** (`scratch/x288/trial/treesize.py`, the watcher's replay at 22:50): 2,084 depth-5 subtrees, 14 depth-4 and 7 depth-3 pending; the 12 depth-5 nodes split so far have 12,580 bottom cases under them (about 1,000 each). So the tree plausibly holds millions of bottom cases: each million costs about 15 days on 10 cores at 12.7 s and 29 at 24.7 s. Rough; the page's ETA for the new pass is still blank.
- **The chain's boxsplit step will not scale as built:** drive() keeps every piece record in memory and scans them all for each leaf it starts, the journal takes about 143 records (some 25 KB) per leaf, and handoff288 loads the driver's journal whole. Fine for 240's 606 leaves; at hundreds of thousands of leaves it means tens of GB of journal (the data volume has 64 GiB free) and more memory than the Mac has. It must be reworked before the first pass ends.

720:11 and 1008:11 found and recorded (Claude, 2026-10-27 22:55-23:30 PDT; dreeves asked for "the optimal thing to do to minimize the time until the next new result", and that operational choices not be asked about).
- **Search:** `scratch/w1008/` holds a copy of scratch/w11b/w11.py (the order-11 antisymmetric tool that found 816:11 and 1024:11) with check11.py and verify11.py. Neither length had been tried: 1008 entered the chart today and 720's earlier attempts used other designs (join720). Preflight (nrand=0, kapcap=3e8): 720 dim 106, best configuration log10 E 0.76; 1008 dim 154, log10 E 10.58. Search seed 1: 720 at CVP rank 145 (31.7 CPU s), 1008 at its first CVP (123.9 CPU s).
- **Checks:** each witness by the search's exact power sums and check11, by verify11.py (plain integers, antisymmetry, (x-1)^11 exactly), and by overnight_v5.exact_order on the decoded page code. verify11 negated 720's signs so that 0 is a +1, as the page's sets have it.
- **Recorded** by `scratch/w1008/apply720_1008.py`: mstar.NEW 720 (11, 14) and 1008 (11, 13), PAGE_CODES p720 (after p656) and p1008 (after p992), PAGE_SET_KEYS; index.html's cells 11–14 and 11–13, example entries in 816's form among the examples in order of n (their join entries removed), SClaude[11][720] and SClaude[11][1008] after 816's, and the changelog's 2026-10-27 math entry "11 ≤ m*(720) ≤ 14, 11 ≤ m*(1008) ≤ 13" after dreeves's entry of that day. mstar.js changed at those two columns only; pow2.js is unchanged.
- **Quals:** new quals_mstar q_examples_at_720_and_1008_raise_their_lo and quals_index q_examples_at_720_and_1008_have_their_entries_codes_and_sets (red first); q_gray_columns, q_lengths_from_1002_to_1024 and the 1024 and 816 entry quals updated where they pinned 720's or 1008's old values or neighbors. quals_mstar 33/33, quals_index 87/87, quals_pow2 16/16, quals_pow2_page 21/21.
- **preview.png** rebuilt at zoom 0.43: dreeves's note between the new-results tables had grown a line, and at 0.44 the legend's last line was cut off.
- **Powers of 2:** `scratch/wish4/wish4.py scan E` (wish3's search, mstar.js cut to its first 1000 columns for p2v_search.atoms, 1008 and 1024 added as blocks at order 11) reproduces every first and undecided K for e = 1..10 exactly: neither new block moves a Theorem 1 threshold.
- **288's bottom budget is 1000** since 22:58 (plan10k's model; the chain relaunched, 432:12 ran 6 s between), with its progress qual updated; watcher restarted.
- **Next:** a symmetric order-12 version of w11 (pinned residue profiles), preflighted at 528-1008. expect.py's local-CLT counts (10^23 at 528 to 10^86 at 1008, 10^11.7 at 432) ignore forced residue profiles: the 2026-10-26 estimate at 432 that accounts for them was 10^-13.6. Order 12 at 768 would lower the e3 and e5 firsts (wish3).

Symmetric order-12 search, w12 (Claude, 2026-10-28 00:00-01:00 PDT; dreeves: "next highest EV thing to try?", and operational choices are Claude's). `scratch/w12/w12.py` is scratch/w1008/w11.py ported to symmetric order 12: M_ORDER 12, s = +1, go800f's Instance.__init__ replaced in memory by sym_init (D_s = D_r; a pin row, coefficient 2 per pair, for each self-mirror class mod 27, 5 and 7, which go800f's s = -1 code never pins), derive's mod-27 objective counting the self-mirror class's pair mass, no 800 special case, check12.py and the symmetric witness test; `planted(n, a)` builds a known sequence's own instance for the positive control. verify12.py is verify11.py for order 12 and reproduces the page's p480 code exactly. Quals: `scratch/w12/quals_w12.py` 4/4 (check12 on p480, an altered p480 and p432; derive at 1008 symmetric and in the order-12 lattices mod 27, 9, 5, 7; every self-mirror class pinned; the planted p480 consistent, with exact R2 > 0 and its free signs in the enumerated coset).
- **Preflight** (nrand=0, kapcap=3e8; best configuration's log10 E, lattice dim): 1008 5.82 (153), 912 2.50 (137), 768 -2.03 (113), 672 -4.98 (97), 624 -8.06 (89), 576 -12.27 (81), 528 -14.95 (73). |H| = 3^25 everywhere, so climb and repair find only 15 to 33 consistent configurations.
- **Searches:** the first runs stopped before any CVP: setup (profiles and ranking, 300+ CPU s) exceeds w11's default budget of 300 CPU s. Relaunched with budget=10800 wall=21600 cap=1200 (1008) and budget=14400 wall=28800 cap=1200 (912). 912's best shape needs about 2e10 nodes per CVP for E*p 0.2 (about 10 CPU minutes per CVP at the 3e7 nodes/s 1008:11 showed). A witness at 912 also moves the excess-3 first from 2^30 to 2^29 (wish3).

Tracked searches (Claude, 2026-10-28 evening; dreeves: "can we rearchitect things so that always happens easily and automatically?", then "those names are fine, go ahead" to `track.py` and `runs/<name>.jsonl`). Before this, each search type needed its own survey function in progress.py (9), a renderer branch in index.html, its own qual file (10) and twice a whole wrapper watcher; w12 and w13 were invisible.
- **Launch every search** from the repo root as `python3 -B track.py run NAME N:M [--found FILE] -- COMMAND ...`: NAME of letters, digits, '.', '_' and '-', one run per name; N:M the question m*(N) >= M?; FILE the search's result file (a witnesses jsonl), whose lines count its results. track.py writes `runs/NAME.jsonl`: a start line (name, target, program, command, cwd, found, adopted, pid, tracker, time), written aside and linked into place so it appears whole, and an end line with the exit status when the command ends; SIGTERM, SIGINT and SIGHUP are passed on to the command. A process started without it: `track.py adopt NAME N:M PID [--found FILE]` (identity by pid and ps start time, polled every 10 s, end status null). runs/ is git-ignored.
- **The watcher** reads every runs/*.jsonl each snapshot (progress.main, so the wrapper watchers carry it) and makes one survey per target (survey_track): running while a run lives, frozen while none runs and one is stopped, else ended; outcome found/none once every run has its end line, else null (shown aborted); one dot per run, in the sea's colors (settled green for ended, running orange, pending gray for frozen, open crimson for aborted); work counts runs by state and the found files' lines; no ETA. The page shows it with the existing attempt view and two new templates (progress-track, progress-track-hint), dispatched by the run's tracked field.
- **Branch audit:** track.py: its mode dispatch, the run options' found conditional, the adopt loop's identity test; survey_track: conditional expressions for each run's state, the search's state and outcome, and a missing found file (no line); progress.main: the records loop; the page: one dispatch (`if (run.tracked)`).
- **Quals:** quals_track.py 5 (start and end lines, the script as program, SIGTERM passed on, adopt's end when the process is gone, refusals of a reused name, a bad target and a dead pid); quals_progress +3 (survey_track's states, its end and outcome, js_text's fields); quals_index q_progress_shows_a_tracked_search. 720 search upgrade in progress (Codex, 2026-10-28 22:02; dreeves: "ok, go ahead and improve the search"). Final measurements and launch metadata will be appended below.

720 upgrade deployed (Codex, 2026-10-28 22:17): `scratch/w12/w12.py` now uses `repair_triples.py` and `repair_lookup.py`. The triple count is the degree-3 coefficient of the product of the orbit rook polynomials; geometric gaps sample unrestricted triple ranks, then compatibility filtering preserves the original independent Bernoulli law. Seeded samples change. The lookup sorts query hashes and searches right bounds only for exact hits, then restores query order; its match bounds and traversal are identical to the old lookup. Every original exact configuration, radius and witness check remains in use.
- **Measurement:** one complete paired 720 repair at the identical climbed configuration and repair seed took 73.8795 CPU s archived, 60.0081 with the new sampler only, and 17.1638 with both changes (4.304 times faster). The last two arms returned identical configurations, scores and statistics; all returned configurations passed both consistency checks and exact rational score checks. These are bounded measurements, not a completion forecast. Component and full-round results: `scratch/w12/bench_repair_results.json`; original and qualified sources: `scratch/speed720-20261028/`.
- **Independent control:** the final search at 768, seed 2, recovered an order-12 symmetric witness at pool rank 11 (12 CVPs, 219.9 total CPU s). No known witness coordinates entered the search. `verify12.py` checked both shifted power-sum systems, symmetry and exact polynomial divisibility; root `overnight_v5.exact_order` checked the decoded code. Records and verification: `scratch/speed720-20261028/control768-final-s2/`. Final seed 1 completed 410 empty CVPs; seed 3 was stopped after the control succeeded. The earlier sampling-only control was superseded before enumeration, with its partial log retained.
- **Execution:** `track.py` validates and exclusively reserves a name before launching, owns a new worker process group, forwards TERM/INT/HUP, and kills remaining owned descendants even after ordinary parent exit. Failed publication leaves a reservation for review. `seeds.py` stops peers and exits nonzero on a failed seed, cancellation or exception; it sets absolute niceness 15. The previous Babai recentering assertion remains: a recurrence now stops visibly instead of silently replacing the failed seed. `progress.py` marks nonzero exits aborted; an ended search with a witness still reports found despite failed or canceled peers.
- **Live:** supervisor 24758 (`scratch/w12/run720.py`) owns driver 24763, seven seeds from 78 onward, at the previous search settings. `scratch/w12/seeds720.pid` names the driver; `scratch/w12/seeds720-fast.log` is the new log. `scratch/speed720-20261028/intent.json` freezes 21 local source files, archived in `final-sources/`; do not edit those inputs while this run is active. The supervisor holds `scratch/w12/run720.lock`, waits for driver/trackers to finish cleanup, then resumes the identity-checked 288 process group 45578 and appends its resume record. Stop via SIGTERM to the driver or supervisor. The 432 lease remains owned by the existing 288 wrapper. No crash auto-restart was added.
- **Quals:** 115 distinct quals passed: 10 launcher, 7 seed-driver, 7 supervisor, 6 sampler, 3 lookup, 4 existing w12 and 78 progress. Two process-inspection quals needed the approved unsandboxed environment. The full progress run exposed a quadratic fixture, `made_up_box288`; it now streams only its first two distinct qualifying keys, preserving the same cases and expectations. That fixture has a new bounded-read qual, and all remaining quals passed. Logs and coverage: `scratch/speed720-20261028/verification.json`. The single final watcher is PID 20245, logging to `scratch/speed720-20261028/progress.log`; verification uses process inspection and loopback HTTP only.
- **Branch audit:** new explicit production branches are the sampler's empty-count return; track's child-exists and unpublished-start cleanup checks; seeds' optimized-Python refusal and main guard; and run720's optimized-Python refusal, child-exists cleanup and main guard. Lookup adds none. Progress adjusts existing conditional expressions. One fixture-only branch stops after the second key. No proof-fingerprinted sources or mathematical bounds changed, and no mutating git commands were used.

Live progress was not broken: the watcher (PID 20245, restarted by Astra at 22:12) writes fresh snapshots; 288 shows running with no ETA yet in its new pass (older than Astra's work), 432:12 frozen, 720:12 ended found (91 runs: 90 ended, 1 aborted, 1 found, 3:32:28), 768:12 ended found.
