Reviewed: track.py (exclusive reservation before launch; the command in its own process group, cleaned even after a normal exit; signals recorded as negative statuses; one small risk: after a normal exit the group is signaled once its leader is reaped, so a reused pid is possible though unlikely within the 2 s grace), progress.py (a nonzero exit marks a run aborted; an ended search with a found line reports found despite failed peers; one docstring line runs past 120 characters), seeds.py and run720.py. All their quals pass when rerun: quals_track 10/10, quals_progress 78/78, quals_w12 4/4, quals_seeds 7, quals_run720 7, quals_repair_lookup 3/3, quals_repair_triples 6/6. Astra's claim "every multiple of 16 from 3264 on admits a symmetric witness of order at least 12" was checked in memory only, with no saved artifact; Claude confirmed it independently: [W, S, −W] with W antisymmetric of order >= 11 and S symmetric of order >= 12 is symmetric of order >= 12 (its polynomial is W(x)(1 − x^(|W|+|S|)) + x^|W| S(x)), and from the page's codes (antisymmetric 432, 720, 800, 832, 848, 880, 896, 928, 944, 976, 992, 1024 at 11; symmetric 480, 768, 816, 912, 1008 at 12) every multiple of 16 from 3264 to 20,000 is reachable and 3248 is the largest that is not, Astra's cutoff exactly.
- **720:12 was found** by Astra's relaunched search (seed 89, 22:27) after its last message; verified by check12, verify12.py and overnight_v5.exact_order; recorded by `scratch/w12/apply720.py` (as 1008's and 816's: replaces the order-11 p720 and SClaude[11][720], cell 12–14, changelog "m*(720) ≥ 12"); quals_mstar q_examples_at_720_and_1008_raise_their_lo and quals_index q_examples_at_720_and_1008_have_their_entries_codes_and_sets moved to it.

OEIS section (Claude, 2026-10-28; dreeves: "can you make a section that links to all the relevant OEIS entries and notes any discrepancies?", README's [OE6] and [OEA]):
- **The section:** `section.oeis`, right after the table of smallest lengths, a Claude block quote styled by `.smallest`'s rules plus `.oeis ul`. It compares the five entries on the problem (A323395, A306248, A323610, A323629, A323614) with mstar.js, then lists eight related ones (A382832, A382833, A362039, A010060, A106400, A001969, A000069, A140047). Its numbers and date are static: quals_index q_oeis_notes_hold_on_the_chart asserts each against mstar.js, so a result that moves a(11), a(12), m*(288) or L6/L7/L8 turns it red and the text needs updating.
- **Discrepancies found:** Stan Wagon's comment on A323395 (2026-10-17, "by D. Reeves and S. Wagon") gives a(12) ≤ 864 and a(13) ≤ 960, one order above what is known with the entry's definition (n-powerful = order n + 1): the doublings give order 12 at 864 and order 13 at 960, so a(12) ≤ 960 and a(13) ≤ 1920. Its a(10) ≤ 432 and a(11) ≤ 480 hold. A323629 omits 112 from its terms, comment and Heinz's g.f.; BGPW's Theorem 3.1 and Tables 1–3 (arXiv v1 and Math. Comp. alike) and A306248's own a(56) = 7 have it. Everything else agrees: A306248's 95 terms, and A323610's and A323614's terms and comment descriptions through 1024.
- **The paper's versions:** arXiv v1's Table 5 has no 112 example; the published version's Table 5 adds 7 112 A5994DB29B45A0 (the page's p112). mstar.BGPW_T5 transcribes v1, so mstar.EXAMPLE_LABELS's 'BGPW Table 5' for (112, 7) holds only for the published version.
- **Sources:** `scratch/oeis/`: the entries' JSON and b-files and OEIS searches as fetched on 2026-10-28, compare.py and report.json, the published paper's text (reprint.txt, from LittlewoodPolynomialsReprint.pdf), arXiv v1's HTML, and shot_oeis.py (screenshots at 1100 and 390 px; at --window-size=390 headless Chrome lays the page out 500 px wide, innerWidth 500, and the shot keeps its left 390 px, so quals_pow2_page's q_phone_has_no_sideways_scroll, with CDP's device metrics, is the phone check).

The page's math in LaTeX (Claude, 2026-10-28/29; dreeves: "could you convert all the math on the page to latex, if you can do it very carefully and can be sure you're not introducing any errors?", README's [KAT]):
- **What changed:** index.html writes its math as `\( … \)`, drawn by KaTeX 0.16.47 (2026-05-16) from jsDelivr: its CSS and two deferred scripts in the head, each with its SHA-256 integrity, auto-render with only the `\(`/`\)` delimiters and throwOnError false, so a bad formula shows in red. `.katex { font-size: 1.1em; }`. A line breaks inside math only after a relation or operator, as in TeX: a forced no-wrap made the page 227 px wider than a 390 px phone, since several formulas had been separate nw spans and KaTeX spaces operators more widely.
- **848 fragments:** every nw span of math, every run of math tags (`<i>` of one letter, `<sup>`, `<sub>`), and the math typed as text: Greek, ±, √, ℤ, +1/−1, sets and number vectors, ellipsis lists, number equations, and the intro's hand-spaced rows, whose `&nbsp;` became `~`. The record-gaps table's script writes its cells as `\(…\)`; auto-render runs after it, since the head's deferred scripts wait for parsing.
- **Not converted:** the changelog's ASCII entries (dreeves's, monospace), the zoom buttons' − and the sentence naming them, the hover readouts, chart labels, the progress run headings (filled at runtime), attributes, `<code>`/`<pre>`.
- **The checks:**
  - `scratch/latex/verify_tex.py` reparses every fragment's LaTeX with its own symbol table.
  - `verify_dom.py` loads the page in headless Chrome with the real KaTeX and compares the MathML of all 951 expressions (848 in the text, 103 in the record-gaps table) with the original HTML: each character, italic or upright, and super/subscript nesting. Result: 0 mismatches, 0 KaTeX errors, no `\(` left.
  - The one allowed difference: lowercase δ, ζ and τ, upright in the HTML, are italic, as LaTeX writes them. Thousands commas (`46{,}656`) are braced so math mode doesn't space them. KaTeX's MathML writes the bar | as U+2223, as an mi (mord), while \mid's ∣ is an mo (mrel); the check tells them apart.
  - Positive controls: three planted corruptions all caught in the browser; quals_latex.py 4/4.
- **Recovery:** the conversion is a pure substitution. Putting back each fragment's HTML (fragments.json) gives the old page byte for byte, and `scratch/latex/index.before.html` is that page.
- **Code that read the old markup:** mstar.page_sources (n cells, the ≥ 52 cell, code labels; mstar.js rebuilt byte-identical); quals_index (41 pattern rewrites, plus cell_n and bounds reading both forms, because the changelog quals parse past commits); quals_pow2_page, quals_pow2, quals_mstar, quals_lattice_search; quals_record_gaps (cells read from KaTeX's TeX annotation); q_scripts_load_data_then_draw (now expects KaTeX's two head scripts); the harness's n-label measurement (the first drawn text node, KaTeX's hidden MathML skipped). preview.png rebuilt at zoom 0.43.
- **From now on,** new math goes in as LaTeX in these forms (memory equipow-chart-updates). The old scratch `apply*.py` recorders write HTML and need converting before reuse.

288:10's missing ETA (investigation 2026-10-28 23:51, scratch/eta288/): progress.estimate returns nothing when a depth has pending fresh nodes but none solved fresh in the current pass (progress.py:859). In the 1,000-budget pass, depths 4 and 3 have 10 and 7 pending and 0 solved: they are the bottom of the depth-first stack, reached last. So no ETA appears until a depth-3 node closes, near the pass's end. The earlier note above ("until a subtree finishes in it") was wrong. The workers run at nice 20, swap was 27.3 of 27.6 GB, and the load about 100.

LaTeX look (Claude, 2026-10-29; dreeves: "the math looks weirdly slightly bigger than the surrounding text. can you research best practices here and find something that looks really good?", then "in some of our other projects -- maybe github.com/bsoule/expost and more recently the help popup of github.com/dreeves/tauction -- we've used some css to make a webpage have a LaTeX feel. can you do something like that for this?"):
- **A correction to the note above:** `.katex { font-size: 1.1em; }` never applied. KaTeX's stylesheet loaded after the page's `<style>`, so its own 1.21em won. The KaTeX link now comes first.
- **Fonts:** the text is Latin Modern, the four woff2 faces of latex.css 1.14.0 from jsDelivr (expost's LaTeX.css). STIX Two Text follows it in --serif for ◆ ● ✦ ≥ ↺, which Latin Modern lacks. The body is 19px (expost's size), tables stay 17px, and `.katex` is 1em: KaTeX's fonts are Computer Modern, so its letters and digits match the text's.
- **Setting:** prose is hyphenated, and justified only from 40rem up, since a phone's justified lines opened wide gaps around formulas. The changelog, the progress section and the code lines stay ragged. The title and byline are centered, the title in regular weight.
- **Glued text:** Chrome breaks a line after KaTeX's inline blocks, so a period could start a line. Auto-render's onload now wraps each formula and the text right after it ("13.", "+1s") in an nw span; `.nw > .katex` still breaks inside the formula.
- **Tried and dropped:** MathJax 4.1.3 with its STIX Two Math font, to match the old STIX Two text (`scratch/mathfont/`), and KaTeX's MathML-only output, which Chrome spaced wrongly.
- **Quals:** quals_pow2_page's q_readout_subscripts_in_page_font compares each subscript's font with the one that draws a probe of the readout's first family (DevTools calls Latin Modern's font LMRoman10). quals_index's q_scripts_load_data_then_draw pins the new onload.

288 paired speed benchmark (Codex, 2026-10-29; dreeves approved the comparison): isolated prototypes and artifacts are in `scratch/speed288-20261029/`. No production search source, journal, or process was modified or signaled. The benchmark has finished; its driver and disposable children have exited. `plan.json` freezes 13 source hashes and 24 cases from 24 distinct visited depth-five parents (12 unresolved at the 1,000-LP search budget, 12 already excluded), selected deterministically from a journal prefix. This is a sample of visited branches, not the whole remaining search. Source copies, red/green qual logs, all completed arm artifacts, and results are retained. All 36 quals and the existing `boxsplit.control()` passed before measurement; the frozen source hashes also matched afterwards.
- **Alternating first-moment cut:** `alternating.py` derives an integer direction from the exact lattice and emits a complete `D,L,L` proof only after unchanged `certbb.verify` accepts it. A previously selected hard specimen closes in four LP calls, but the broader 24-case comparison found no new exclusions: both arms closed 12 and left 12 unresolved. CPU totals were 34.906124 versus 34.848733 seconds. This provides no reason to insert the cut into every production case. The existing solver's budgeted LP count omits unsuccessful width probes; CPU time is the timing measure.
- **Boundary certificates:** `boundary.py` checks the nearest excluded residue count on each side, verifies the certificate's monotone slope, and checks it again at every farther excluded count. `bench.py` supplies those certificates through the existing boxsplit interface, preserving every piece and its exact checker. Eleven of twelve whole-subtree comparisons finished; both arms closed the same pieces with identical verdicts and identical non-outside records. CPU totals were 34.697891 versus 30.453670 seconds (12.23% less CPU, 1.139 times faster). All eleven candidate arms were faster; counted LP solves fell from 3,370 to 1,895. Case 09 hit the pair's 90-CPU-second limit during its first, candidate arm, before either arm completed, so it supplies no paired timing or exclusion. The unchanged production searches have no new limit.
- **Timing limits and next candidate:** two low-priority disposable processes ran at once; arm order alternated. Shared direction preparation was outside timed arms, but some other caches still favor the second arm. The sample does not establish a universal speedup or a completion ETA. `certbb.solve` constructs `Lp` before consuming supplied certificates, so the prototype still constructs an unused LP for each outside piece, plus its boundary models. Avoiding that construction is a concrete unmeasured follow-up. The prototype asserts if a boundary does not yield the required root certificate; it has not been deployed. `analysis.json` includes all 24 cut pairs; `summary.json` reports timings only for pairs where both arms closed.
- **Branch audit:** the arithmetic prototype has three explicit branches (missing range, non-thin interval, uncertified sides); the sampler has five (partial line, bottom-record filter, latest eligible record, balanced distinct-parent selection, main guard); the harness has five (cut success, saved witness, live-child cleanup, peer cancellation on witness, main guard). Boundary preparation has no explicit `if` statement and one predicate skipping empty sides. No production branch was added.

288:10 speedup deployed (Codex, 2026-10-30; dreeves: "can you just keep working and spawning sub-agents until you get 288:10 running faster?"):
- **Change and measurement:** `scratch/x288/certbb.py` is an isolated copy of the root solver, changing only HiGHS `log_to_console=False` and `output_flag=True`, both asserted. On 24 frozen cases from distinct visited depth-five parents, an ABBA comparison of the actual staged source took 69.090157 CPU seconds before and 61.719781 after (10.67% less CPU, 1.1194 times faster). All 24 verdicts agreed, including 12 unresolved and 12 excluded cases; each arm ran twice. Every exclusion passed the unchanged exact checker. This measures those cases, not the whole remaining search or a completion ETA. Benchmark, frozen source hashes and raw measurements: `scratch/speed288-deploy/staged/`. The arithmetic, lazy-LP and boundary-certificate prototypes in sibling directories were not deployed.
- **Preserved work:** the stopped journal's 742,573 records (742,527 distinct keys) were preserved byte-for-byte. `scratch/speed288-deploy/journal-migration/journal.original.jsonl` is its exact 192,343,089-byte archive; the manifest records both headers, full hashes and body SHA256 `94ed7adac916c1952879cb8f0ab3d65bab1a59effac3d3b5f98467dbbb3e184f`. Only its active header changed, explicitly naming that legacy archive. Existing exclusions retain their old provenance; they were not re-proved by the new producer. New CODE: `77821325eee02749daf68276469556bb98df1963`. The local runner's SOURCE now also freezes `scratch/x288/legacy.json`. Root proof sources and certificates, including the sources used by the paused 432 search, are unchanged. `installed-sources.json` and `qualified-sources.json` in the deployment directory pin the deployed inputs; do not edit them during the run.
- **Live processes:** old guardian 45565 and watcher 96810 were stopped deliberately; every member of search group 45578 ended, and the old guardian resumed the same nine 432 processes before the replacement paused them again. New guardian 4324 owns tracker 4329, shell group 4335, overnight driver 4340 and ten workers, all at niceness 15 with one BLAS thread. The tracker record is `runs/288-10-a2-1793343864456995000.jsonl`. Stop through the guardian so tracker cleanup precedes resuming 432. The existing overnight/boxsplit/handoff/overnight chain and LP budgets are unchanged. The startup control passed at 00:06:19 PDT; the new solver recorded its first exactly checked case exclusion at 00:06:36 (6.4 wall seconds). Saved branches are also being replayed; that restart cost must not be mistaken for fresh LP throughput. No new mathematical bound is claimed.
- **Progress:** `run_modules.py` loads each run's actual solver without changing canonical root imports, requires identical geometry/replay/checker functions, and verifies explicit legacy archives. Split and ETA caches were copied to the new CODE keys only after checking the unchanged geometry; old keys and the original cache remain. The single watcher is `search592_progress.py --watch`, PID 4359, logging to `scratch/speed288-deploy/progress.log`. The guardian log is `scratch/speed288-deploy/run288.log`. A fresh snapshot and byte-identical HTTP response on port 5501 were verified without browser or screen access. 288 dashboard restart repair (Codex, 2026-10-30; dreeves: "did you break things? why is the dashboard stale? and what does "no reliable ETA" mean? that's the whole point of the graph, to show the uncertainty"):
- **Failure:** deployment verification checked only the first snapshot. The next refresh blocked while rebuilding 1,573 uncached splits, leaving progress.js at 00:05:51 PDT although the search continued. "No reliable ETA" was incorrect: that snapshot had a median of 6.8 days and a 90% model interval of 3.6–12.8 days for the current overnight pass. Uncertainty belongs in the displayed band; this is not an ETA for later boxsplit/handoff passes or the full proof.
- **Recovery:** progress.recover_splits reconstructs a split only from a single compatible pass's complete child observations, with a distinguishable INFEASIBLE pool. It checks child counts, cross-pass membership, and existing cache ordering; vector children sort numerically and the pool stays last. Incomplete observations still require the original exact split. Actual-log validation recovered 1,554 missing entries without enumeration. The watcher computed the other 19, then saved all 1,619 required cache entries. Every old ETA record and existing cached split was preserved. The bounded scratch survey reached its 180-CPU-second cap after computing those 19 but before saving; it is not evidence of a finished snapshot. The production watcher completed without that probe limit.
- **Accounting:** history_costs retains each job's first observed cost across contiguous passes with identical target, budgets, cascade, and pool budget. It removes overlapping current replay charges from predictive donor totals. Historical work affects the cost samples used to predict new subtrees; it never adds finished work to remaining work. Population counts, draw quantiles, and existing remaining-work aggregation stay unchanged. The phase boundary prevents mixing different search budgets. This repairs restart-induced optimism; it does not independently calibrate the model's interval coverage.
- **Quals:** 21 new recovery/history quals and 18 existing module/progress quals passed together (39 total). Seven integrated existing survey, replay, publication, and runtime quals passed; the publication qual required execution outside the sandbox for os.nice. Independent agents also passed 18 recovery-related existing quals and eight saved-state estimator quals. One existing qual had required an actual split call; after explicit discussion it now requires exact cached children and an estimate, retaining its ended-run/no-work assertion. Red evidence, green logs, source hashes, and independent reviews are under scratch/progress288-recovery/, scratch/progress288-history/, and scratch/progress288-repair/.
- **Live verification:** watcher 4359 was stopped and replaced by the single search592_progress.py --watch process 36792, started 01:59:43 PDT at nice 15. Its log is scratch/progress288-repair/progress.log. Consecutive snapshots at 02:04:38 and 02:07:45 PDT were byte-identical to HTTP responses on port 5501, had advancing search counts, and retained nondegenerate uncertainty bands. First publication took 294.7 seconds; the next arrived 187.3 seconds later, including the existing 60-second sleep. The latter median was 5.59 days, with a 90% model interval of 3.18–9.23 days for this pass. No browser or screen was accessed. Guardian 4324, search driver 4340, all ten workers, all six pinned search-source hashes, and the same nine paused 432 identities remain unchanged. No search was restarted and no journal was rewritten for this repair.
- **Branches:** eight new explicit conditions: four in recovery (compatible pass, wanted parent, observed child of wanted parent, complete closed-pool partition) and four in historical accounting (phase boundary, duplicate old job, above-bottom own cost, overlapping current job). Two recovery comprehension filters select the pool and the nonpool vectors. The estimator adds no branch. No UI copy was added. Historical deployment manifests remain unchanged; scratch/progress288-repair/qualified-sources.json pins the repaired watcher sources, and final-verification.json records the live checks.

Speedup campaign (Claude, 2026-10-30, from 02:30; dreeves: "just go crazy and do all the things one after the other, whatever you can come up with to make the search faster and faster. keep spawning sub-agents, etc."). Sub-agents work in dated scratch directories, each with a NOTES.md; nothing is deployed until reviewed. State at 06:00:
- **Solver, `scratch/speed288-20261030/`:** `deploy/certbb.py` (W1 skips width-test directions already seen wide, F decides the producer's certificate bound in float64 with a rigorous Higham error bound and exact fallback, plus per-process LP models and vectorized ray scaling) used 2.19x less CPU on Astra's 24 frozen cases (95% CI 2.06-2.39) and 2.24x on 240 live cases, no verdict flips; Check, lower_bound and verify byte-identical (reviewed: no producer bug can make a false exclusion, since every token still meets the unchanged Check). Deploying needs a chain restart, a chained header migration (`deploy/migration2.py`) and about 45 minutes of replay (re-splitting 1,356 depth-five nodes). Pending before deployment: a 1,500-case Devex sample (1.13x more, but it changes every proof) and the close fraction at budgets 2k-20k (to decide raising the pass's bottom budget at the same restart).
- **Boxsplit step, `scratch/boxsplit288-20261030/`:** about 431,000 open leaves at 03:00, growing ~10k an hour, ~1.45M expected at the pass's end. Of 16 measured leaves, 3 were heavy (90 to ~1,500 CPU s; their cost is own children's certbb searches); light ones take ~2.5 s. If heavy leaves are 10-20%, the step takes months; the 1,000-leaf survey that measures this was still running. Candidate `boxsplit_fast.py` (no unused LP, boundary certificates carried along each side) is 1.9-4.9x faster on light leaves (2-leaf smoke test); safe to install before the chain reaches its boxsplit step, but the current watcher re-hashes scratch/x288/boxsplit.py every snapshot and would die on the change.
- **Math, `scratch/math288-20261030/`:** Lemma A (2-adic, proved, checked on lattice generators and 38 known sequences): for D^4 = 0, f'(-1) is 4096·(D_1 + D_3)/8 mod 8192, |f'(-1)| <= 20736; class c0 = 4096 (189k open leaves) allows only ±4096, ±12288, ±20480. Slicing those leaves by f'(-1) closes 44% of them (median 72 LP solves) and shrinks the rest's boxsplit count boxes 3-45x; no gain for c0 = 0 (246k). Lemma B (3-adic, f'(ζ3) fixed mod 2K'(ζ3)) adds little. Open leaves have 10-25 width-test directions with only 1-3 lattice values, which certbb ignores: being tried as narrow-direction branching in `scratch/narrow288-20261030/`.
- **Running agents:** the progress watcher rewrite (incremental reading, per-run isolation with stale marks, both boxsplit log forms, surviving source changes), the 432:12 study (`scratch/speed432-20261030/`), narrow-direction branching, and the solver and boxsplit follow-ups above.
- **432:12, `scratch/speed432-20261030/` (finished):** (1) faster producer (rows cached per case, numpy optimal-point test first, float64 bound screen with rigorous error bound and exact fallback; same LP calls, identical trees, asserted on 16 real boxes): 1.40-1.45x, fanout_code 8620858794e904df…faaa, two qual hook edits (`patch_A/`). (2) reversal lex-leader cut: R: p -> 431-p maps each case onto itself (exact row-space test `invariant`); L(x) = sum_k 2^(215-k)(x_k - x_(431-k)), L(Rx) = -L(x), so keep L >= 0: of 70,013 pool boxes 34,393 are dead (dropped, assembly checks -max L >= 1 exactly), 4,034 mixed (start with the cut token ('D', -L, 0), which the unchanged Check accepts); about 49% fewer remaining LP solves; with (1), about 2.8x (page median 104 days -> about 37). Needs dreeves's approval of the lemma in the proof's trusted base and of five qual spec changes (seven edits); fanout_code 3f5e6a88…570d plus `patch/reprove.py` and three progress.py hunks (new pool fields dead and lex). Dry run on a copy-on-write clone matched the offline dead count exactly. Deploy when 432 next runs: SIGTERM the running (not stopped) driver, install, quals, restart the watcher, `fanout.py switch 432`, relaunch with proposal.md's step-7 command. Higher derivatives at -1 and other roots of unity gave no usable cuts; static and frac branching rules no better.
- **Progress watcher rewrite, `scratch/watcher-20261030/` (deployed 04:58 PDT):** progress.py reads only what each file gained (`Reading`, `Tail`, `Log`, `Records`, `Boxes`), keeps the tree and the replay incrementally (`Tree`, `Grown`, `History`), and bounds each run's survey (`bounded`, `LIMIT` 120 s): a run that overruns or fails keeps its last good data, marked `stale` with that data's time (the page shows Claude: "stale" in its summary). A one-minute snapshot costs about 2 CPU s (96 before); output byte-identical to the old code's on frozen copies of the real files; both boxsplit log forms parse; a DST fix for 2026-11-01's repeated hour. A cancelled seed's forked CVP child left such a group, so six trackers died in cleanup with no end line. `group_signal` now treats ESRCH and EPERM alike: it asserts, by `ps -A`, that the group has no live member (new `live(pgid)`), and raises otherwise. Quals: quals_track.py 13/13, with `q_cleanup_passes_over_a_group_of_zombies` and `q_group_signal_fails_loudly_while_a_member_lives` (red on HEAD's track.py, `trackfix/track.HEAD.py`). The new file was installed by atomic rename.
- **The six records** (624:11 seeds 2, 4, 6, 7; 576:11 seeds 2, 7) were closed by `trackfix/closeout.py`, which asserts its evidence first. Each end line has status null, as adopt writes a status track.py cannot read, and its time is the seed log's last write (the tracker's traceback, after the run's process was gone). Then their `.reserved` files were unlinked, in track.run's order. Report: `trackfix/closeout.json`. Their tracebacks show the cancel path, so track.py would have written -15: they now show as ended, where their siblings cancelled with them show as aborted.
- **672:12's seed 35 failure** (`babai/`): reproduced exactly by `capture35.py`, a byte-identical copy of the w12 tool rerun at seed 35. After round 0, |t|^2 = 264, and Babai's answer alternates between w and -w with w = e0 + e1 + e2: an exact tie, which double-precision rounding sends to the other side every round. `recenter` (in w11.py, w12/w12.py and w10s/w10s.py here) now keeps a round only if it shortens t. |t|^2 is a nonnegative integer that falls at every kept round, so the loop ends with no cap and no raise. The `if` replaces the old `if not any(w)`; no branch was added. Quals: `q_recenter_settles_at_a_tie` in quals_w11 (5/5), w12/quals_w12 (5/5) and w10s/quals_w10s, red on the old code (fixture `babai/tie672_s35.json`, exact checks in `babai/tiecheck.py`).

- **Fork vs exec** (`sched/probe_sched.py`): a fork()ed child at nice 15 has scheduler priority 16; an exec'd or posix_spawn'ed one at nice 15 has 31, the default, as every exec'd search process here does. With the parent hot or cold it is the same. At load about 20 the shares were 0.63-0.91 forked against 0.91-0.94 exec'd. With shape (0.6, 1.5, 1.5) the CVP children used 5 CPU s in all over 672's 10,920 CVPs, against about 245 CPU s per seed in the parent, so the tool was not changed.
- **Fork vs exec under the 528 batch's load** (`sched/probe_load.py`, load about 53): a forked child got 0.08 of a core at priority 16, an exec'd one 0.28-0.36 at 25-27. An exec'd CVP child would start at 0.13 CPU s (0.24-1.2 s wall) against the 0.00-0.01 CPU s of a typical CVP, so a per-CVP exec would be slower; a persistent exec'd worker would be the arrangement for heavier shapes.

Order-13 witness agent, 2026-10-31 from 06:05 (Claude; `scratch/w13-1008-912/`):
- **Tool:** `w13.py` there is scratch/w11-624-576/w11.py at order 13 with four changes.
  - `wagner.py`: consistent configurations by Wagner's four-list algorithm. Lists of the cheapest compatible move pairs of four orbit groups; a level-1 key from a homomorphism of H onto a cyclic 3-group; exact class matching. Each pool round repairs a climbed configuration, then polishes its best repair (repairs with d = 0). At 1008, |H| = 3^30: the old two-list repair matched nothing (`before/red_pool.log`); a round (lists of 3e5, 20 targets, 12 CPU s) gives about 300 repairs and 1,800 polished configurations, best q 2.2-3.3 (log10 E -2.4 to -2.6; ceiling -1.94).
  - `cvpworker.py`: one exec'd enumeration process per seed in place of a fork per CVP (forked children are starved at nice 15). A bug of its first version (it raised its own hard CPU limit; seed 3 of the first launch died after 3,262 CVPs) has a red-then-green qual.
  - `reduced()`: BKZ at block sizes 30, 40, 45 after BKZ-20 (option bkz).
  - An unknown key=value option stops the run.
- **Quals:** `quals_w13.py` 15/15 (red on the old tool in `before/`: 5 of 14 passed); `mutants.py` (3 mutants caught); the copied driver quals (seeds 10, run 4, page_check 1, lookup 3).
- **Controls:** the Wagner qual recovers the doubled p480's configuration (960) from an 8-move perturbation. End to end at order 13 (`control960_f11.py`, `control960_f11.log`): the doubled p480 in its own instance with 11 fixed orbits (dimension 75) went through w13's planted instance, reduction, recenter, target and the worker's enumeration; at lattice seed 7, the first whose pruning region (0.6, 1.5, 1.5) held it, the enumeration returned it (order 13 by exact_order and check13) after 70 nodes. At F = 9 (dimension 145) it lies outside every shape tried, in 150 lattice seeds and more. `control-w11/` (the same code at order 11) found a new 528:11 witness at pool rank 556 in 98 CPU s; check11 and plain integers confirm it.
- **Design probes:** fixed orbits F = 8, 9, 10, 11 give |H| 3^7, 3^30, 3^32, 3^33, E ceilings -2.88, -1.94, -10.67, -19.69, lattice dimensions 190, 153, 117, 81 (`fixplan_1008.log`, `fixplan2_1008.log`). w11's 9 stays.
- **Enumeration at dimension 153:** fplll's cost model is unreliable here. The exact tree of a planted target under shape (0.4, 1.5, 1.1) held 7.0e6 nodes in its last 40 levels (`treesize_1008.log`). Linear pruning (0.02, 1.0, 1.0), p_emp 0.8%, lets almost no target past the top levels: 0 of the first 97,222 CVPs took 0.05 CPU s. The order-11 witnesses' tail profiles look like random points' (`control-w11/skew_528.log`, `profile.py`). At 528:11 the same code with linear pruning found a witness after 2,695 CVPs whose summed E*p was 0.00034 (`control-w11/linear/`), so w11's excess over the model (about 3,000 times here) holds under linear pruning too.
- **1008:13 launched** at 07:54:20 (`launch1008.sh`, 3 seeds, 150 seeds or 6 h). Seed 3 died on the worker bug at 07:58 (432 paused 220 s). Relaunched from seed 4 at 08:00:16, 432 paused again (watchdog 2 h). A seed: 60 rounds (30,720 configurations, 12 min), BKZ to 45, CVPs at about 8 ms each.
  - **Cap raised.** Two planted targets under linear pruning were not found within the 1,800 CPU s cap (`plantcal3_1008.log`; the first is rerun with a 14,400 s cap, `plantcal4_1008.log`). So the driver was stopped at 09:00:18, after seeds 4-12: 276,480 CVPs, summed E*p 1.34, none took CPU time, no witness. 432 was resumed then.
  - **Relaunched** at 09:00:28 from seed 16 with cap 14,400 s and WALL 18,000 s (`launch1008b.sh`), 432 paused again.
  - **Note:** the 08:00 pause record was rewritten at 08:40:29 by another agent's re-pause (new 432 pids), and run.py's resume then resumed those.
  - **The basis mattered more than anything else.** At 09:53 the driver was stopped again, after 558,022 CVPs in all: none took CPU time, no witness. On a basis reduced by BKZ 30, 40, 45, 50 alone, planted targets were found in 35 and 73 CPU s under linear pruning and in 11 and 26 s under (0.02, 1.0, 1.05), whose p_emp is 0.108 (`plantcal_alt_1008.log`, about 4e7 nodes a second). On the production's path (BKZ-20 first, then 30, 40, 45, and also 50), planted targets ran past 1,800 s. fplll's model cost told the two bases apart (1.6e4 against about 1e7 nodes for linear pruning).
  - So `reduced()` now runs only the given blocks after LLL (bkz default 20 = go560f's basis), and `cheapest_basis()` keeps the cheapest of `bases` randomized bases by the model cost of the shape. Two new quals were red, then green; 16/16.
  - **Third configuration** (`launch1008c.sh`) from 10:03:22, seeds from 28: bkz 30,40,45,50, bases 2, shape (0.02, 1.0, 1.05), cap 1,800 s, WALL 14,700 s. 432 paused again.
  - **Evidence by 10:40:** seeds 28-30 under the third configuration ran 92,160 CVPs, summed E*p 6.1, none costly, no witness. Seeds 29 and 30 had bases of model cost 1.2e6 and 4.9e6 (planted targets on such bases are found in minutes at most), with summed E*p 4.3 between them. If the model held, P(no witness) would be 1.4%; so at 1008:13 the rate is at most about 0.7 of the Gaussian model (95%), unlike order 11 at 528, where the same code ran 3,000 times above it.
  - **BKZ is erratic** at block 50 without pruning strategies: the cheaper of 2 bases cost 1.2e10, 4.9e6 and 1.2e6 at seeds 28-30; one basis took 597 CPU s and stayed bad (`basesprobe_1008.log`). `reduced()` now takes a CPU cap a BKZ call (option bkztime; fplll checks it between tours), with a red-then-green qual; 17/17.
  - **Fourth configuration** (`launch1008d.sh`) from 10:41:37, seeds from 34: the third with the cheapest of 4 bases and bkztime 120, WALL 12,300 s. The third ran 10:03:22-10:41:29 (432 paused 2,287 s). Through seed 33: 666,303 CVPs, summed E*p 10.3, none costly, no witness, 24,917 CPU s (`summary.py 1008 1-3,4-15,16-27,28-150`).
  - **Fifth configuration** (`launch1008e.sh`) from 11:27:49, seeds from 40: 3 bases, no BKZ cap. The fourth (10:41:37-11:27:48, 432 paused 2,771 s) got 0 good bases of 20 with the cap (model cost 5e8 to 1.6e10); the uncapped third got 2 of 8. Through the fourth, the 1.05 shape alone: 206,177 CVPs, summed E*p 13.6, nothing.
  - **Why five configurations found nothing: a 2-adic obstruction.** Every antisymmetric ±1 sequence of order 13 and length 16k with k odd (1008, 912) is f = (x−1)^13 (x+1)^2 g with g ≡ Psi(x^16) mod 2, so its residue sums mod 16 lie in the coset C_16 = P + 2 L_P(16), P = (x−1)^13 (x+1)^2 (lemma and proof in `twoadic.py`). Every point of C_16 lies far from the binomial center: kappa_16 is about −5.5 at 1008 and −6.3 at 912. The searched cosets were dead. The lattice's top GSO levels are the layers of f(−1) (spacing 632) and f(i) (19.8). At f(i)'s layer, every target sat about half a layer from every coset point (`layer.py`, `layer_1008.log`: summed squared offsets over the top 8 levels, median 116 against 7.8 for planted cosets). Hence 0 of 777,376 enumerations passed the top level. Those runs say nothing about existence.
  - **The fix: a mod-16 pin.** `derive` pins C_16's profile of largest binomial mass (`twoadic.top`), beside the mod-5 and mod-7 pins. `planted` pins the sequence's own sums mod 16. Mod 32 stays free: given that profile, C_32 costs only a factor of about 10^−0.43, while pinning C_32's best profile cut a round's top log10 E from −0.09 to −2.75 (`pre16.py`, `pre32_1008.log`). The lattice dimension at 1008 is 145. Quals 20/20, three new: the lemma on synthetic odd antisymmetric multiples; derive's admissible mod-16 pin; and the pool's coset centers within squared distance 1 of C_16. The last two were red on the pre-pin copy in `before_pin/` (distance² 2,111.6). The 960 control recovers the doubled p480 end to end with the pin (seed 1, 68 nodes; `control960_f11_pin16.log`). The pinned pool's top log10 E is about 0 at 1008 (seeds 47–49) and −4.6 at 912 (`pre/preflight16_912.log`).
  - **Sixth configuration** (`launch1008f.sh`) from 12:54:49, seeds from 47: the pin and 12 rounds, otherwise as the fifth. The fifth (11:27:49–12:31:48, 432 paused 3,839 s) was stopped as futile. The sixth was also stopped at 13:37:29 (432 paused 2,560 s). All 6,144 enumerations of its seed 49 failed at the top level, though their summed E*p was about 20. Each seed's BKZ took 30–35 minutes: BKZ-50 alone took 792 s a basis on this lattice, against 83 s unpinned (`bkzprobe_pin16_1008.log`).
  - **Odd primes too.** With the mod-16 pin, the top 8 GSO levels are exactly the mod-11 residue functionals, and the top 12 hold the mod-13 ones (`toplevels.py`). A sequence of order 13 has its residue sums mod a prime p < 14 in L(p, 13), which is coarse there. The configurations' coset centers sat between its layers: squared distance 769 (mod 11) and 530 (mod 13) from the nearest admissible profile (`before_pin1113/quals_red.log`). With mod 11 and 13 pinned, mod 17 took their place (0.874 of its functionals' norm in the top 8 levels; `toplevels_pin11_13_16.log`). `admissible.py` finds each prime's admissible antisymmetric profile of largest mass directly. `derive` pins every prime in `w13.ODD_PINS`. Two new quals (pins admissible; pool centers within distance 1) were red on `before_pin1113/`, then green.
  - **ODD_PINS = (11, 13, 17).** With 19 and 23 as well, go800f's Instance did not finish in 10 minutes at a load average near 200. With 17 it took 225 s (`timeinst_17.log`): rows 42, dimension 126, log10 E ceiling 6.44. Across one Wagner round, 41 of 64 configurations pass the top level's bound (summed squared offsets over the top 8 levels, median 8.1 against 4.3 planted). The remaining top level (√r 7.45) lines up with f near the 9th roots of unity (`toplevels_pin_to17.log`).
  - **Seventh configuration** (`launch1008g.sh`) from 14:42:59, seeds from 51: pins mod 11, 13, 16 and 17; 12 rounds; the cheapest of 2 bases; WALL 3,000 s, the rest of the 6 hours. The qual suite was rerun beside it (`quals_green7.log`: 22/22). The 960 control recovers the doubled p480 with every pin (dimension 48, seed 1, 1 node; `control960_f11_pinall.log`).
  - **End of the 1008 budget** (15:33:01; 432 paused 3,002 s for the seventh, 21,437 s in all). The seventh's top configurations had log10 E about 3.2, but its bases' model cost for (0.02, 1.0, 1.05) was 9e12–7e13 nodes. Each linear shape costs 1e11–4e13 for p ≥ 2e−4 (`shapescan_lin_1008.log`). Of its 9 enumerations, 6 failed at the top level, though their summed E*p was 834. The other 3 passed and were still enumerating at the wall. So the pinned model is not honest yet. The remaining top level (√r 7.45) lines up with f near the 9th roots of unity, not with any residue functional tested. Probably f′(ζ_9) ∈ (1−ζ_9)^12 Z[ζ_9], that is, the class-wise first moments mod 9, which no pin fixes. In total: 53 seeds in 7 configurations, 906,406 enumerations journaled, 50,197 CPU s, no witness (`summary.py 1008 1-3,4-15,16-27,28-33,34-39,40-46,47-50,51-53`). The first six configurations' cosets could hold no witness near their centers, so their nominal E*p of about 57 is no evidence of absence.
  - **912:13 was not run;** 1008 used the 6 hours. With all pins its dimension is 110 and its pool's top log10 E is −2.4 (`pre/preflight_pins_912.log`), against 1008's 3.2. `launch912.sh` predates every pin.
  - **Next step, if resumed:** align the configurations with the top structure (pin the first moments by class mod 9, or rank configurations by their top-level Babai offset), then use far cheaper pruning, since E is about 10^3. Each restart needs about 4 minutes for the instance (go800f's congruences) and 10–25 minutes of BKZ.
  - 912:13: `launch912.sh` uses 60 rounds and predates the pin.

- **Sub-second pause record** (2026-10-31 11:12, Claude):
  - **Cause:** pause432.py names a done record by its resume second, truncated. A pause shorter than a second (pause432.1793466190.done.json, 10:03) ended "before" its paused_at, so progress.in_order asserted, and 432's survey went stale from 10:03 on.
  - **Fix:** the ready288 agent's proposal, applied. A done pause ends at max(R, paused_at), and the record must satisfy R + 1 > paused_at. - **Search allocation as a bandit** (2026-11-02 23:48 PST; dreeves: "faster search sounds good; 768:13 and 720:13 would be awesome. but be systematic about this. can we model this as a multi-armed bandit problem?"). Two Claude sub-agents:
  - **`scratch/bandit-20261103/`: the model.**
    - An arm is (open cell, method). Its rate λ = θ·r: r is measured as model E·p per CPU-hour from preflights, and θ's prior per method family is fitted on past runs' exposures and successes (Poisson).
    - Rewards are one-shot and arms deteriorate as they fail, so the index is the posterior expected results per CPU-hour.
    - 432:12 is the deterministic arm, by its remaining CPU.
    - Speedups are valued as rate multipliers.
  - **`scratch/pinned-fast-20261103/`: faster fixed-sign generation** for searches/pinned (the README's "1000 times faster (C++, incremental coset updates)"). It works on a copy, with exact coset checks kept and quals red first.
  - Neither launches long searches or touches 432's processes.

- **[SYM] follow-up: the Symmetry Conjecture for m ≤ 11, CLAIMED and pending review** (2026-11-03, `scratch/sym2-20261103/`, NOTES.md; quals cover 16/16, bounds 6/6, verify 2/2, mutants 15/15).
  - **The claim:** C(m) holds for every m ≤ 11 and every n, except possibly (288, 10), given cited bounds:
    - m*(144) ≤ 8 and ≤ 9 (BGPW Table 2);
    - m*(272) ≤ 8 (BGPW's ILP, uncertified);
    - m*(304) ≤ 8 (ours);
    - order-11 bounds at 240, 288, 384 and 752.

The 528:12 campaign was stopped first (SIGTERM to driver 90204 at 14:15:29; its journal ends `end, reason signal`). The first launch, at nice 15, ran 14:16:25 to 14:25: the workers got 3.5 cores in all. ftree.py forks its workers, and on this Mac a forked child's base priority is 31 minus the parent's nice, while exec'd processes keep 31 at any nice (a probe: nice 0, 5, 15 gave forked children 31, 26, 10). Relaunched at nice 0 at 14:27: 6.6 cores, and the Mac's idle CPU fell to 0%. Driver 50691; record `runs/formtree_288_10_b.jsonl` (the first launch's is `runs/formtree_288_10.jsonl`); memory guard PID in `scratch/formtree-mac-20261104/memguard.log`.

Then `track.py adopt NAME 288:10 DRIVER` under a new NAME (a name is one run), and `scratch/formtree-mac-20261104/memguard.py DRIVER`.

It wants `'python' in basename(args[0])`, and Homebrew's interpreter is `.../MacOS/Python`. handoff.py's `running()` uses the same test, so `handoff.py save` would not see a live run here. Use `pkill -TERM -f 'ftree.py 288:10 top_288_10.json.gz'` from a shell whose own command line doesn't contain those words. memguard.py instead matches progress.runs's interpreter-then-script pattern; its control stopped a stand-in ftree.py and spared the shell, which its first version had killed.

Codex found both by reading `proofs/fkernel.cpp`; `scratch/fkernel-fix-20261104/quals_fkernel_fix.py old|new` confirmed them by running it, red on the old checker and green on the fix:
    - (1) `node_name` printed `(ll)value[id]`, so a node pinned at v and one at v ± 2^64 shared a name (three nodes, one name).
    - (2) An early `return false` in `weights`'s row loop left `GMon[M]` set, and later certificates in that process left modulus M's rows out of W while K kept them: after one refused task, the old checker **accepted a forged closure** a fresh process refuses.
    - Neither acted in the run: every verdict was accepted, so no early failure happened, and pinned values stayed at most 1024.
    - **Fix** (`fkernel.cpp` 4715f227…): `str128` in `node_name` (the same string for every value under 2^63), and `weights` clears `GMon` on entry. Honest streams give byte-identical verdicts old and new.

    - Codex's driver guard (`ftree.py` 84905926…): a task refused, misnamed, with the wrong spill count or a Python disagreement or exit now stops the driver before any spill move. A recorded `py=False` blocks a resume. So the README's "REJECTED … runs again on resume" no longer holds: the run stops at the first refusal.
    - Its Mac-safe `stopall.py`, `handoff.py` and new `processes.py`, so `python3 stopall.py ftree.py` works here now.
    - Its assembly guard in `proofs/formtree.py`: `assemble` refuses `py: false`.

- **1456:12 tried, then proved impossible** (Claude, 2026-11-07 00:20–01:20; dreeves: "ok, next?"; `scratch/wish1456-20261107/NOTES.md`). Every length ≤ 1024 prime to 3 has hi(n)/log2 n ≤ 11/log2 800, which caps powers-of-2 constructions. 1456:12 was the one length prime to 3 up to 2400 whose order passed the 3-adic chains above that cap.
  - **What it would have done:** the page's decider (p2v_search64 over the 57 atoms plus 1456:12) found that a 1456:12 block would move the excess-4 first from 36 to 31 and the excess-7 first from 56 to 51. Both witnesses were checked exactly; excess 9 and 10 were TOO_BIG.
  - **The search:** four 30-minute tilted seeds (tracked as tilted_1456_12_s11..s14) found nothing. A second round (s21..s24) refused at setup.
  - **The proof:** order 12 at 1456 forces every position ≡ 0 mod 3 to one sign, which put that class into the free set with tilt ±1. A degree-11 integer-valued P with P(i) ≡ 0 mod 729 off that class, but summing to 243 mod 729 over it, excludes order 12. certcheck.py checks it independently. So m*(1456) = 11.
  - **Tooling:** the scratch copy `forced/tilted.py` lets a tool run with forced coordinates (quals_forced.py, controls 544:10 and 1008:13). It is not installed. The round-1 cosets were empty.
  - **On the page** (2026-11-07; dreeves: "nice, are you putting that in index.html?"): a paragraph in the Asymptotics section's Claude block, before "Whether U is finite", gives the proof with ψ and links `proofs/proof1456.py`. - **288:10's rate is exponentially weighted** (Claude, 2026-11-07 04:00; dreeves, after the ETA's spikes were traced to hours when other work borrowed the search's cores: "maybe 3 hours at least, or just do an exponentially weighted moving average of rates or whatever seems elegant").
  - **What the spikes were:** on the 1-hour rate, the graph's spikes match rate dips in run288v.log. The worst was Nov 6, about 19:30: 649–890 nodes/s against about 2,700, and a median of 14.6 d where the weighted rate gives about 3.7 d.
  - **The change:** formtree_work.read's rate is the throughput over the current driver's whole log, each interval weighted by exp(-age/TAU), with TAU = 3 h. Ages run between interval middles back from the newest, so the newest interval's weight is exactly 1.
  - **Quals:** new quals_formtree_work test_rate_weights_recent_hours_exponentially, red first (10/3600 where 0.0016183 was due). quals_formtree_work 16/16, quals_formtree_eta 19/19, quals_work_progress 7/7, quals_chance 29/29, quals_progress 124/124.
  - **Not changed:** the model's ±35% log-normal rate uncertainty and its file. runs/models/288_10.json's assumptions.rate text still says one-hour aggregate; it is descriptive, and editing it would change the model's digest.
  - **Watcher:** restarted as PID 45826. Earlier ETA points keep their old rate; the history is not recomputed.
- **Symmetry section: where the conjecture stands** (Claude, 2026-11-07; dreeves: "can you be clearer in the symmetry conjecture section about how close we are to proving it?"). A four-item list follows the opening paragraph of the Claude block:
  - orders up to 8, proved by BGPW;
  - orders 9 to 11, proved here at every length but 288 at order 10, given the listed bounds; four of those are BGPW's uncertified integer programs, and one, at 752, needs a rerun;
  - order 10 at 288, being decided by the form-tree run;
  - orders 12 and up, open.
  The TODO recap was extended. The list holds no math of the forms quals_symmetry parses. quals_symmetry 5/5, and the phone, spacing and curly-quote quals pass.
- **Symmetry section: the large-length theorem** (Claude, 2026-11-07; dreeves: "sure", after asking for an attempt by hand). Three paragraphs end the Claude block.
  - **The theorem:** given Freiman and Litsyn's theorem as BGPW quote it (not read here), with s = (−1)^m and 2^a the least power of 2 above m, the conjecture holds at every sufficiently large length iff some s-symmetric order-m sequence has a length that is 2^a × odd. The proof is p ∨ s·p* at lengths divisible by 2^(a+1), p ∨ q ∨ s·p* at 2^a × odd, and the converse from the theorem.
  - **What the page supplies:** 912:12, 1008:13 and 1440:16 give orders 12, 13 and 16. Orders 14 and 15 lack an example: every explicit sequence of order 14 or more, and every join and product of them, has a length that is a multiple of 32 (the chart has only 960 with lo ≥ 14, past it only 1440).
  - **Chat only:** why no full hand proof is in reach. At 288, the conjecture plus BGPW's computation means m*(288) = 9, a nonexistence that residue and norm tests don't reach.
  - **Quals:** the status list's last item now points to the theorem, and the TODO recap was extended. quals_symmetry 5/5, quals_derivations 7/7, and the anchor, phone, curly-quote and spacing quals pass.

- **CPU usage chart** (Claude, 2026-11-07; dreeves: "could we get a nice CPU usage graph on the search page? similar to what activity monitor shows. three colors: one for the search, one for all other processes on the machine, and one for idle, if any", then "maybe aligned with the ETA graph").
  - **The sampler, `cpu.py`:**
    - It reads the kernel's busy and idle ticks (host_processor_info via ctypes, as Activity Monitor does) and each tracked run's process-group CPU time (`ps -A -o pid=,pgid=,stat=,time=`).
    - Every 60 s it appends `[t0, t1, idle, {run name: share}]` to `runs/cpu/intervals.jsonl`.
    - **Run it** from the root with `nohup nice -n 15 /opt/homebrew/bin/python3 -B cpu.py --watch >> runs/cpu/sampler.log 2>&1 &`. A lock (`runs/cpu/lock`) refuses a second one. Started 06:52 as PID 59474.
    - **Not managed by the shepherd or the watcher:** relaunch it after a reboot.
    - It is separate from the watcher because every directory the watcher writes is pinned by q_watch_rewrites_and_sleeps, and because snapshots take seconds to minutes while samples keep a steady minute.
  - **The watcher:** `progress.cpu_series` reads the file through `progress.cpu_file()`, read-only. It maps each interval to hours of running with `running_before`, the ETA chart's x. It keeps only intervals naming one of the run's records, merges them down to at most CPU_POINTS = 480 rows, never across a gap, and emits `cpu` for every run (empty where never sampled).
  - **The page:** each run box has `.progress-cpu` right under its ETA chart, with the same x scale through `etaX(run)`, extracted from etaChart. It stacks search (`--progress-spent` blue), other processes (#b5651d rust) and idle (`--progress-hair`); both colors pass the dataviz validator. Its key reads "CPU usage: search, other processes, idle" (dreeves's words), and the readout gives the latest or hovered row's percentages.
  - **Quals:**
    - quals/quals_cpu.py, 8 new: ps time formats, interval math, refusals, live ticks, groups, the sampler loop with a failure, one sampler at a time.
    - quals_progress, 2 new: q_survey_track_carries_its_cpu, q_cpu_series_merges_to_at_most_cpu_points. Key sets of 2 quals gain cpu (spec change, dated).
    - quals_index, 1 new: q_progress_draws_cpu_aligned_with_the_eta_chart (band edges at the ETA records' xs). The fixture runs gain cpu.
  - **Mishaps fixed along the way:**
    - An early version sampled inside the watcher, and quals that run `progress.main` wrote junk intervals into the live file; those were dropped.
    - q_watch_rewrites_and_sleeps has a pre-existing load-dependent race when run alone: its fixture's `subprocess.run(timeout=600)` busy-waits with `time.sleep(0.001)` into the qual's global sleep stub.
  - **First reading:** the 288 search got 44–58% of the CPU, with other processes (another session's crashla quals at nice 0) taking the rest and nothing idle.
  - **Layout** (dreeves, the same morning: "let's keep the blue/red/purple line of numbers directly below the ETA graph, not below the CPU graph", then "not the black text line though"). A run box now reads: ETA chart, status line, Lindy and stages notes (they say "the above graph"), CPU chart, then a tracked search's counts line ("4 runs: …").
    - The counts line is a second `p.progress-status[data-track-line]` after the CPU chart, filled by trackView; empty, and so hidden by `:empty`, for other runs.
    - workView's status-line selector excludes it, so the chart boxes' briefs are unchanged.
    - The CPU qual checks this order.
- **288:10's ETA stale from 13:04 to 14:28 on 2026-11-07, fixed** (Claude; dreeves: "the search page is stale right now?", then "can-opener question?").
  - **Cause:** Astra's v2 work model refused every snapshot because its importance sampler gave fewer than its 1,000-effective-draw floor: about 860 of 65,536, falling as the search narrowed the posterior.
  - **Fix:** formtree_eta.attach now doubles the proposals until the effective draws reach the model's floor (1,000 for v2, 0 for v1), up to PROPOSALS_CAP = 2^20, and still refuses past it. On the live run, 131,072 proposals give about 1,575 effective draws, at 0.2 s.
  - **Quals:** new test_proposals_double_until_the_effective_draws_reach_the_floor (red at 819, then green). quals_formtree_eta 20/20, including Astra's pinned 65,536 fixture and the degenerate-proposal refusal.
  - **Watcher:** restarted as PID 63934.
  - **Pitfall:** `pgrep -f "search592_progress.py --watch"` also matched a leftover `bash -c` whose command text named the watcher, and the kill hit it too; the CPU sampler, its child, survived (PID 59474). Stop the watcher by the pid in scratch/watcher-20261030/launched.json, or with progress.runs-style matching.
- **288:10's dots are its tree's roots** (Claude, 2026-11-07 17:00; dreeves: "remind me why we aren't doing the sea-of-dots visualization of that kind of thing?", of the ETA's plunge as root 8 finished, then "the words you're adding on the page are not helping. it needs to just be clear from the visualization").
  - **The picture:** a search with a work model has, in place of its runs' dots, a block per root of its tree, in the model's order. A dot is a fixed number of nodes: the power of 10 nearest the model's prior of the tree's nodes over `SEA_ROOT_DOTS` = 1000, so 1M nodes at 288:10. Each block is the root's nodes checked in settled dots, then the model's median of its nodes left in pending dots (open once the search ended; one dot while the model makes no forecast). A complete root has at least one dot and an unfinished one at least one left. Keys are `288:10 i`. The runs' line below the CPU chart still counts the runs.
  - **No words:** the readout has no hint for these dots. Template progress-roots-hint holds only a hidden span, the canvas's label for a screen reader (Claude-attributed, TODO). dreeves's "One dot per run" hint stays for every other tracked search.
  - **Code:** formtree_eta.attach returns `roots` (its work's: name, done, complete) and `posterior` (draws's weighted draws, unread there, None while the model makes no forecast). progress.rooted takes each root's weighted median of nodes left from the posterior (`weighted_median`, interpolated as attach's scatter quantiles are), makes the sea, and drops both. trackView picks the hint by `run.work_eta` (one conditional expression). rooted has two conditional expressions (open vs pending; no forecast). A first version read `samples['trees']` inside attach, which broke two quals_formtree_eta quals whose fake draws have no trees; the pass-through leaves them unchanged.
  - **workView's status row:** since this morning's CPU chart it selected `.progress-status:not([data-track-line])`, which quals_work_summary.cjs's fixture (keyed by the literal `.progress-status`) could not answer: red since then. It now takes the first `.progress-status`, as statusLine does, which the qual accepts unchanged.
  - **Live, 16:55:** 665 dots checked, 392 left, the left dots the sum of the roots' medians (the total's median left is larger, since medians do not add). Root 7: 143 checked, 75 left; root 4: 0 and 213.
  - **The model ignores the queue:** in the 5 big finished roots, once a root's queue had 11 or fewer tasks, at most 16% of its nodes remained (1% or less in 3 of them); at 100 tasks, 35–71%. Root 7 had 11 queued tasks at 143.4M checked while the model gave it a median of 75M more, so its finish should drop the ETA again (by about 0.5 day at the median if it ends within 1%). Measurements: `scratch/root-dots-20261107/`.
  - **Quals, red first:** quals_work_progress test_each_root_is_a_block_of_its_nodes; quals_index q_progress_shows_a_search_s_roots_without_words. Red evidence: red.log (the first, worded design) and red2.log (HEAD's page; rooted reduced to HEAD's behavior).
  - **Watcher:** restarted as PID 35740.
- **Times on the progress page** (Claude, 2026-11-07 evening; dreeves, of 288:10's box: "redundant elapsed time here. let's keep the one in blue and nix the one in black", then "show elapsed time with full granularity like so: 3d 4h 5m 6s", "make it update second by second", "no need for the "so far"", "sane number of sigfigs for everything else based in a principled way on the amount of uncertainty", and "mark this with "HMSFORMAT"").
  - **Elapsed:** page/progress-section.js's `hms` (marked HMSFORMAT) writes every time so far, the snapshot's age and a batch's elapsed as "3d 4h 5m 6s": from the largest unit reached down to seconds, zeros between kept, U+202F between parts. `duration` (the colon form) is gone. The status line's running form ticks each second: statusLine pushes [its element, data time − so_far] onto `live`, and `tick` writes hms(now − since) into each still-connected one. The ETA chart's tip and the pool tip stay static (past records). The clock is `wall`, which the quals freeze through `window.PROGRESS_CLOCK` (quals_index's PROGRESS_FROZEN, as PROGRESS_POLL_MS sets the poll). A stale running run ticks from its data's time.
  - **Removed words:** "so far" from template progress-line and the frozen form; "elapsed …;" from template progress-track. The batch templates keep their elapsed, the only one in those views.
  - **Rounding:** u = half the width of the interval to go (`spread`), shared by the to-go, in-all and finish figures. A duration is rounded in its unit to the place of u's first significant digit, never coarser than a whole unit (`places`, `figure`, `about`, `range`). A clock time goes to the largest of the hour, ten minutes or the minute that u reaches (`STEPS`, `stepOf`, `rounded`), the hour written "12pm". u is at least 1 s. dreeves's examples come out exactly ("16 h–3 d", "Sun 12pm – Tue 10pm").
  - **Spacing research:** `&thinsp;` is U+2009 (Unicode line-break class BA, so lines may break there); U+202F has no named entity (class GL, no break). In Latin Modern bold they measure 0.20 and 0.19 em. CLDR's English narrow duration (`Intl.DurationFormat`) is "3d 4h 5m 6s" with U+0020 and no zero units. Evidence: `scratch/hms-20261107/`.
  - **Quals:**
    - new, red first: quals_index q_progress_elapsed_is_days_hours_minutes_seconds_and_ticks and q_progress_rounds_figures_to_their_uncertainty;
    - spec changes, dated: q_progress_shows_a_tracked_search (no elapsed in the counts), q_progress_names_a_run_s_later_stages (7–9 d, 10–14 d) and the status-line, tip, brief and pool quals' expected strings;
    - Python mirrors: `hms`, `step_of`, and `ampm`/`moment`/`finish` taking u;
    - fixture changes: the four attempt harnesses (the page's own `fill`, `hms` in place of `duration`) and quals_work_summary.cjs (`hms`).
  - **Branches:** `ampm`'s minutes-or-not expression is the one new conditional; `tick`'s `made === null` early return replaces the old `if (made !== null)`; `live`'s isConnected filter is a predicate.
  - **Suites afterwards:** quals_index 165/165, quals_codes 16/16, quals_derivations 7/7, quals_pow2_page 37/37, quals_progress_recaps, quals_search288_progress and quals_sweep288_progress OK, all 11 Node harnesses. quals_focus_progress fails test_page_section_has_no_scope_prose, as it has since 6000eaa (2026-11-04 22:41). That commit's work-model line (Latin prose: samples, nodes checked, nodes/s, source) brought back the prose and numbers the qual has forbidden since cb92b81 (dreeves, 2026-10-23: "i don't want a bunch of prose and numbers, i want the blue/red/purple graphs"). No later note records running the qual. dreeves cut that line on 2026-11-06 ("2. kill the whole line with number of samples") but asked for the work bar and "1. add nodes/s to the blue/purple line", and those are the two matches left: `data-work-measure` and `data-work-rate`. The fix, a dated spec change (dreeves: "yes, that qual change sounds acceptable"), reads the section's attribute names, comments left out. It allows exactly those two `data-work-*` names and forbids the four removed attributes as whole names. Renaming the two attributes would have turned it green only by dodging it. `scratch/hms-20261107/focus_qual_check.py` runs the changed qual on four pages: the current one passes; 6000eaa's, cb92b81's parent and the current one with `data-work="lp"` put back all fail. quals_focus_progress 18/18.

The border between blue and brown steps once per row, about seven rows to a pixel, and the outline traced every step: 1,076 of 5,040 band pixels white at 1x, 3,866 of 20,590 at 2x (`scratch/hms-20261107/cpu_seams.py`). The page's key shows an entry per status in run.statuses and hides the rest. No block can be split (a node that splits is checked like any other) or running (work in flight is unknown until its task ends, and under one million-node dot: ten tasks of at most 240 s each).

- **152:7 stopped** (Claude, 2026-11-09 13:07 PST; after dreeves asked whether its little value meant "BGPW already established it, without proof, via ILP?"):
  - **Why:** 152 is not one of BGPW's ILP cases. Those are 168, 176, 184 and 272 (scratch/oeis/reprint.txt line 583, in §5 after the proof that 272 ∉ L9: "When n = 168, 176, 184, and 272 we used ILP, which cannot be certified correct").
  - **The hand proof:** Berend and Golan (Math. Comp. 75, 2006; `scratch/app-20261105/sources/berendgolan2006/bg2006.txt`, case 1.e) proved m*(152) = 6 by hand in six lines:
    - |P(−1)| ≥ 128 forces the mod-2 sums to ±(64, −64), so 70 of the 76 even positions are in A;
    - mod 3 forces (−27, 27, 0), so exactly 12 of the 51 multiples of 3 are in A;
    - but the multiples of 6 already put more of them in A than that. (They count 25 multiples of 6 in [0, 151]; there are 26. The contradiction holds either way, and with the opposite sign too.)
  - **The other "earlier work" bounds:** they say their negative results are by hand except N = 160, so 96 and 144 were not rigor gaps either. Their form-tree proofs are independent second proofs.
  - **The stop:** tracker 61658 first, then process group 92484 by SIGTERM; then `track.end` wrote status −15, so the page shows it aborted.
  - **State at the stop:** 2.319e8 nodes, 0 refused; 18,300 verdicts and 2,459 pending tasks kept in `scratch/certify-20261109/152_7/run/`.
  - **To resume:** `scratch/certify-20261109/cell.sh 152:7 152_7 7`.
- **Postmortem and Berend–Golan follow-up** (Claude, 2026-11-09 afternoon; dreeves: "oops, what's your postmortem on almost wasting so many CPU hours? what else needs to be double-checked in light of this? how much further can we take the techniques in Berend & Golan?"):
  - **Waste:** about 110 CPU-hours (152:7 about 90, two stuck 152:6 controls about 20). Memory `read-sources-before-computing` records the lesson.
  - **Table 2 citations for upper bounds:** the chart takes exactly eight from Table 2: 56, 96, 144, 152, 168, 176, 184, 272.
    - 56, 96, 144 and 152 have printed proofs by hand: Boyd, and Berend and Golan's §4 and cases 3.a and 1.e.
    - 168, 176, 184 and 272 come from BGPW's ILPs. Form trees now prove 176 and 272, so only 168 and 184 lack a checked proof.
    - Berend and Golan's one computer-dependent bound, m*(160) ≤ 7, the chart proves itself (residue mod 9).
  - **Page:** `scratch/bg-20261109/apply_handproofs.py` corrected the Symmetry section and its TODO recap to say so (backup `index.before-handproofs.html`).
    - dreeves had rewritten the two new changelog lines and removed "TODO:" from their comments. quals_index's changelog() then refused them, since it skipped only TODO comments; it now skips any one-line comment. That is a spec change, with a dated comment in q_changelog_sits_under_the_authors_as_in_freshat.
    - Checks: red on his edit, green after. A junk line in the changelog is still refused, and an arbitrary comment is accepted.
  - **Berend and Golan's 152 argument as an LP screen** (`scratch/bg-20261109/bg_lp.py`, float HiGHS, a screen, not a proof):
    - **The cut:** at N:7 with N = 8 × odd, Lemma 4.5 and Prop. 4.6 force f(−1) = ±128. Then one LP per sign over [−1, 1]^N with the start node's class sums mod 3, 5 and 7.
    - **Results:** 152:7 closes all 1,264 start cases; 168:7 closes 3,461 of 3,484 (23 left, with the moment rows); 184:7 closes 10,029 of 10,030 (1 left). Those two are BGPW's remaining ILP cells, which dives had put at 4.9e9 and 1.8e12 nodes.
    - **The prover's gap:** the form tree's parity coset at modulus 4 already holds f(−1)'s grid 128 + 256Z, yet its 152:7 run never closed start cases this way. With 4 first in the want order, 10 of 20 sampled start cases still took over 60 s (`scratch/eta152-20261109/order_B_4first.json`). Why is not yet known.

The Symmetry Conjecture at every sufficiently large length (Claude, Fable 5.2, 2026-11-09 PST; dreeves: "can you take a stab at proving the Symmetry Conjecture?"). Everything is in `scratch/symlarge-20261109/` (NOTES.md first); the page is unchanged.
- **Proved:** for every order m there is an n0(m) such that every length n ≥ n0(m) with period(m) | n has a (−1)^m-symmetric sequence of order at least m, and one of order exactly m. So BGPW's conjecture can fail at only finitely many lengths at each order. The count of such sequences is (1 + o(1)) 2^(n/2) (2/π)^(d/2)/√det M, d = ⌊m/2⌋.
- **Not proved:** the conjecture. n0(m) is 2^O(m²) (about 2^31 at m = 2, 2^1062 at m = 12), so the lengths below it, the open cells among them, are untouched.
- **Method:** the right half of a symmetric sequence under ⌊m/2⌋ linear conditions in an integer basis of polynomials; the conditions' parity obstruction is exactly Divisibility (Lucas); the count as a Fourier integral; a lemma that phases near integers in mean square come from one integer-valued polynomial (finite differences on a long run, then Markov and Chebyshev), which leaves one major arc. It uses neither Freiman and Litsyn's theorem nor any example.
- **Checks:** quals_symlarge.py 18/18 (11 red against stubs first), every mutant caught; two fresh agents reviewed the text with their own code and found no error in the lemmas or the assembly, only wording, all applied (`review_fixes.py`; `proof_as_reviewed.md` is what they read). Remark 5 (exact order) was added after the reviews and has two quals of its own.
- **A qual's expectation was wrong and was changed:** count over main term within 10% at m = 5, N = 48; it is 2.07. At m = 5 a minor arc decays like cos(π/16)^N, so the main term is no guide at lengths a count reaches: ratios 0.55 to 3.6 at m = 5, 0.04 to 2.5 at m = 6. The local-CLT counts used earlier as heuristics are such main terms.
- **Incident:** review A's first script asked numpy for 294 GB; the kernel killed it at 16:36:41 at 76.7 GB compressed, with 206 idle daemons. No panic; the watcher, the CPU sampler and shepherd kept running; swap stayed at 5.4 of 6.1 GB an hour later. Port 5501 was not listening at 17:20, cause not established.
- **On the page the same evening** (dreeves: "yes to putting it in index.html attributed clearly to Fable 5.2", then "please also add a changelog entry for this"): the proof is `proofs/symlarge.html` (typeset HTML, its one source), with `proofs/symlarge.py` and `quals/quals_symlarge.py` (24 quals: the lemmas' finite parts, the remarks' numbers, the page's math, phone width and links). index.html got a changelog entry, a sentence on the status list's last item, and a second block quote at the end of the Symmetry section, attributed "— Fable 5.2"; quals_symmetry got two quals for it, red first. quals_symmetry 8/8, quals_derivations 7/7, quals_pow2_page 37/37; quals_index 149/168 and quals_layout 5/6, each with the failing set it had before the edit.
- **Found red, not caused by this:** since dreeves's rename of the same evening (b5ce25c: "Opus 5.5" for "Claude" in titles, legend and attributions), mstar.py's NEW table no longer matches the page, so `mstar.page_sources` asserts, mstar.js cannot be remade, and quals_codes (0/16), quals_mstar (38/48) and 19 quals of quals_index fail. Reported to dreeves, not changed.

Credits as keys, fewer word-pinning quals, and a sweep that was overkill (Claude, Fable 5.2, 2026-11-09 night; dreeves: "probably yes to the decoupling but i didn't understand everything you said there so be careful. i stand by the point about quals enforcing specific prose on the page being pretty useless."). Notes: `scratch/keys-20261109/NOTES.md`.

It found 28 word-pinning quals in quals_index, about 9 in quals_pow2_page, 2 in quals_derivations, 2 in quals_lattice_search and 1 in quals_416_page; quals_search288_progress and quals_search592_progress had word checks in plain sight. All are cut or loosened, each with a dated comment: a qual that held a sentence now holds its figures, the text without its words (`figures`, `among`, with a qual of their own), reads a label from the page, or finds the thing by its place. Checks dropped outright: dreeves's paragraph between the new results' two tables is no longer read; the lattice section's list need not say "complete" or "no sequences"; a pin-tree entry need not hold a `Name: “`; two progress templates need not say "no completion estimate"; the 416 proof need not say "BGPW".

The script and its suite came back out of the tree (they are in `scratch/keys-20261109/`); no further run was made; eighteen suites were never swept. The rule now: when a prose edit turns a qual red because the qual pins wording, cut that check, then.

Not touched: quals_index q_remarks_name_the_chart_s_constructions matches no sentence of the page any more and checks nothing.
