In the quals, two conditional expressions (quals_pow2_page's reason_holds: a credit is compared as it stands, anything else by its figures; quals_derivations's thue_morse: the page or a given text, as derivations has it) and the filters of a few comprehensions (figures, numbers, the phrase for find in page).

Every suite run once, and the red ones made green (Claude, Fable 5.2, 2026-11-09 night to 2026-11-10 01:30; dreeves: "ok, let's get the quals green (never by just deleting or weakening a qual unless we know absolutely the qual is wrong)"). Notes: `scratch/green-20261109/NOTES.md`.
- **The run:** all 87 suites in turn, 23:30 to 00:53 (`scratch/green-20261109/run.py`, `first.out`): 84 green. Red: quals_focus (1 qual), quals_pintree (2), quals_codes (1). By then dreeves had committed the credits and the word-pinning cuts of the note above (6b299ec).
- **quals_focus, a stale number in the qual:** test_known_witnesses_survive_exact_rows_and_lp asked that mstar.build(672) have order 11. It has had order 13 since 2026-11-04 (ced8c1b, the 672:13 sequence), by its exact power sums. The pair is (672,13) now, with a dated comment. The exact rows and the LP accept the sequence at 13; the rows refuse it at 14, and refuse a copy with two entries swapped at 13. 10 of 10.
- **quals_pintree, two causes:**
  - q_the_prover_keeps_real_sequences ran a process pool from `python -c`. Where processes are spawned, as on macOS, the workers cannot import a -c script's function, so each died and the pool waited for ever. It runs FOLLOW from a file now; what it asks is unchanged.
  - Both prover quals need highspy, which this Python lacks. pip installs it only with `--break-system-packages`, which Claude Code's auto mode refused, so the install is dreeves's. From `scratch/green-20261109/venv/bin/python` (the system's packages plus highspy 1.15.1) both pass, in 286.6 s and 203.8 s. In the usual Python they are red until highspy is there.
- **quals_codes, the display's sleep:** q_boxes_open_after_their_blocks waited 500 s once, to its driver's watchdog; alone it passes in 32 s. The display had turned off at 23:35:59, seconds into that browser's run. Measured with `display.py`: when the display goes to sleep, a running headless Chrome's page turns "hidden" within 2 s, draws no frame, and its timers tick once a second; with `--disable-backgrounding-occluded-windows` it stays visible at 60 frames a second. A Chrome started while the display already sleeps is not affected, which is why the suites after it passed that night.
- **The fix:** quals_index's `HEADLESS` holds the flags every headless Chrome of the quals starts with, that one among them. chrome() uses it, and the node drivers of quals_pow2_page and quals_codes get it as `C.headless`. New qual, red first: quals_index.q_every_headless_chrome_starts_with_the_same_flags (one line of quals/ names the headless flag). The qual that hung passes with the display put to sleep in the middle of its run (`through_sleep.py`). The display was woken three times for 4 s each for these trials (01:08, 01:11, 01:14).
- **Suites after:** the eleven that start Chrome, all green (`browsers.out`: quals_codes 16, quals_derivations 7, quals_index 171, quals_lattice_progress 12, quals_mstar 49, quals_pow2_page 37, quals_progress_bar 2, quals_proof_layout 1, quals_record_gaps 7, quals_symlarge 24, quals_symmetry 8); quals_focus 10, quals_layout 6. So all 87 are green but for quals_pintree's two prover quals.
- **highspy, that morning:** dreeves had no preference between the user site and the scratch pylib folder beside fpylll, and asked "what's most consistent and anti-magic?" The user site: of the third-party packages the tracked Python imports, PIL, sympy, mpmath and ortools are there and numpy and scipy in Homebrew's site-packages, all on Python's own path, and only fpylll is in pylib, reached by `PYTHONPATH`; there nothing in the code changes and no qual's result depends on an environment variable. He installed highspy 1.15.1 there at 08:38 (`pip install --user --break-system-packages --no-deps highspy==1.15.1`; the wheel lays down `highspy/` and its dist-info and nothing else). Both prover quals then passed in the usual Python with no `PYTHONPATH` (294.4 s and 205.3 s, `scratch/green-20261109/logs/pintree_provers_usersite.log`). Every suite's latest run is green.
- **Branches added:** none.

The README's [FTP]: an exposition of form-tree proofs on the page (Claude, Fable 5.2, 2026-11-10; dreeves: "can you start tackling the tasks in README one at a time when you're done with the quals?"; the task: "add exposition, attributed to Claude, that defines and describes form-tree proofs"). Notes: `scratch/ftp-20261110/NOTES.md`.
- **The page:** a `<section class="formtree" aria-labelledby="formtree-title">` right after the pin trees' section, a Fable 5.2 block quote with a TODO recap: forms and grids, why some weights have coarse grids (a single residue sum mod a prime p > m has G = 1; the Legendre symbol's weights have G a multiple of p once m > (p − 1)/2), the tree (close, pin, split; the top; reversal), three steps of `searches/formtree/fixtures/small.txt` as an example, certificates, what checks a proof, and the proof at 288's sizes. `page/style.css` names `.formtree` wherever it names `.pintree`. A changelog line, `Fable 5.2: “Exposition on form-tree proofs”`.
- **Numbers, each recomputed** (`scratch/ftp-20261110/totals.py`, `steps.py`): the proof at 288 assembled from its snapshot by proofs/formtree.py has 16 start nodes, 205,491 tasks, 981,147,930 nodes (a spilled node counts in both of its tasks, so 980,942,455 distinct), 466,813,767 splits with 2.10 children each on average, 1,470,167,061 pins without a split and 4,902,209,550 certificates; the Python checker read 1,841 tasks, of at most 14,448 nodes and 1,569,215 nodes in all (a sampled stream over 64 MB is not given to it).
- **Quals, red first:** `quals_index.q_form_trees_have_their_exposition` (the section and its heading; its links, each to a file or folder in the repository or to an id on the page; its numbers with a thousands separator, each one of the proofs' counts; each `<li data-step="k">`'s numbers, those of the stream's k-th bound step as the checker reads it; no sentence asked for) and `q_form_trees_section_is_styled_as_the_pin_trees_is`. `scratch/ftp-20261110/mutations.py` runs the first on changed copies of the page: 16 changes that must turn it red do, 6 of wording do not.
- **[MAI] bit:** the first draft linked `fixtures/small.txt`, and quals_codes.q_texts_show_their_boxes turned red ("the page links no .txt file"). The text now links the fixtures' folder and names the file.
- **Review:** a fresh agent checked every claim and number against the checkers' code and the data, with its own proofs and code (`scratch/ftp-20261110/review/`): no wrong mathematics and no wrong number; nine findings of scope and wording, all applied (the prover's forms are short dual vectors at prime moduli only; certificates are not tight, the prover stops each LP early; the 1,841 tasks are the smaller ones; four minutes is the 288 run's limit; the top's 139 certificates are kept and formtree.py checks them; "nonzero squares"; "orbit" for "class"; terms defined at first use).
- **By-product, the prover's forms:** at the moduli 4, 8, 9, 16, 25 and 27 the prover reads its forms with their classes permuted (`cellconfig.dual_forms` makes them in the digits order, `pex.write_config` writes them so, `pex.cpp` reads weights by class). That is why it missed the f(−1) cut at 152:7, the first puzzle of `searches/formtree/closing-step.md`, which now records the answer: with the weights put by class, 17 hard 152:7 start cases close in one node each; the first levels of the trees at 480:14 and 480:13 change by a few percent at most, so the open cells' estimates stand. No proof is affected. `write_config` is not changed yet. Notes: `scratch/forms-20261110/NOTES.md`.
- **Suites:** the 28 that read the page, its stylesheet or the form-tree files, all green on the final text (`scratch/green-20261109/ftp3.out`; quals_index 173).
- **Branches added:** in the new qual only: the filter that tells a link into the page from one into the repository, and a grid's offset counted among a step's numbers only when it is not 0.

The README's [SYM]: the symlarge proof inside index.html, folded (Claude, Fable 5.2, 2026-11-10; dreeves: "thanks! go ahead on [SYM]"; the task: "i think i'd rather have this symlarge proof in index.html, but default collapsed"). Notes: `scratch/sym-20261110/NOTES.md`.
- **One copy, in the page:** the proof moved out of `proofs/symlarge.html` into a fold of the Symmetry section's block quote that states the theorem, right after the paragraph about the proof: `<details class="proof-exposition" id="symlarge">`, `<summary>The proof</summary>`, `<div class="proof-body">`, the markup of the page's other typeset proof. No `open` attribute, no script: in Chrome, find in page and a link to one of its sections (`#symlarge-s0` to `-s6`) open it, and a link to `#symlarge` lands on it closed (tried in headless Chrome). [MAI] asks for folds "instead of linking to separate pages", and the 512 proofs' text files went the same way; Astra's 384 proof, a copy beside its own page, was not the model, since this proof has no files of its own to stand with.
- **The old address:** `proofs/symlarge.html` is ten lines that send a reader to `index.html#symlarge`. A dead link would have made the move worse in one way (the Pareto rule).

Two changes by hand after it: Remark 5's long formula is a displayed one (its right side is one piece 313 px wide, and a phone's column in the fold is 309 px), and Remark 2's `<code>quals_symlarge.py</code>` is a link (in the Symmetry section a `<code>` is a sequence's code, and quals_symmetry counts them).
- **Stylesheet:** `.symmetry ol` joins the rule that spaces the sections' lists (the remarks ran together), and the rule gained `grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr)` (a list is a grid, and an item holding a displayed formula grew as wide as the formula: 613 px in a 390 px frame).
- **Quals, red first:** quals_symlarge's three quals of the proof's page now hold the fold (q_proof_draws_all_its_math, 500 formulas; q_proof_fits_a_phone; q_proof_links_resolve), with two new ones (q_proof_is_a_closed_fold_of_the_symmetry_section, q_proof_s_lists_space_their_items); 26 of 26. quals_symmetry finds the block quote by its fold and reads its numbers outside the fold; q_symmetry_large_lengths_links_its_proof is q_symmetry_large_lengths_holds_its_proof. Each has a dated spec-change comment.
- **Two reds that were dreeves's commit d67f6dd, not the move:**

Narrowed, with a dated comment: a bare mention passes when the page has that float, by his own rule that the paper's carry the BGPW prefix and the page's are numbered from 1; a mention of a float the page lacks still fails.

It reads Chrome's port file as soon as the file exists and asks once, so a file not yet written, or a port not yet answering, fails it. Not changed.

The README's [REF]: the last scripts out of the root (Claude, Fable 5.2, 2026-11-10; dreeves: "maybe this is a good time for task [REF]? and anything else we can identify for task [REO]?", then, of the layout proposed: "name ideas: searchwatch/ instead of watch/; pagegen/ instead of page/; for data files, does a data/ directory make sense? let me know if i'm thinking of any of those wrong."). Notes: `scratch/ref-20261110/NOTES.md`.
- **Names:** `searchwatch/` and `pagegen/` are his. For the data files he asked whether a `data/` directory makes sense; answered yes, as `pagedata/` (greps cleanly, sits beside `pagegen/`, and a bare `data/` would look like the searches' data too), one rename from `data/` if he prefers that.
- **Moved, with plain mv (no git):**
  - `proofs/`: proof384.py, proof384.wl. `quals/`: quals_proof384.py, quals_proof384_page.py (they had stayed at the root for the 384 proof's own page, now a redirect).
  - `pagegen/`: mstar.py, pow2.py.
  - `pagedata/`: mstar.js, pow2.js, progress.js. index.html loads them from there.
  - `searchwatch/`: progress.py, search592_progress.py, sweep288_progress.py, track.py, shepherd.py, shepherd.log, shepherd.err, cpu.py, chance.py, formtree_eta.py, formtree_work.py, run_modules.py, stages.json.
- **How a moved script finds things:** HERE (cpu.py's ROOT) is the directory above its own, the repository's root, so every path built from it is as before; stages.json and shepherd.log are found beside the script.
- **Importers:** `scratch/ref-20261110/rewrite_imports.py` turned the line that put the root on sys.path into one line per directory needed, in 38 files; six more by hand. The root has no module left to import.
- **By path, not by import** (what a move like this breaks without an import error):
  - four code fingerprints of finished searches named a moved file by its old path (join720.py, search288.py, search592_geometry.py: `'pagegen/mstar.py'`; pruning288.py: `'searchwatch/progress.py'`);
  - quals that copy a script into a sandbox: quals_track (the copy goes into the sandbox's searchwatch/, so that its records land in the sandbox's runs/ and not in the directory above it), quals_pow2's -O qual, quals_chance's band probe, quals_progress_modules (it took progress.py's directory for the root);
  - the watcher's launcher and the preview builder, in scratch; `.vscode/settings.json`'s Live Server list (`pagedata/progress.js`) with quals_live_server.cjs.
- **Staging the page in quals:** quals_index has `PAGEDATA`, `DATA`, `POW2` and `data_file(d, name)`, the place of a data file beside a copy of the page; 30 lines of quals_index and eleven other suites use it (`scratch/ref-20261110/pagedata.py`, each replacement exact, its count asserted).
- **The page:** its two script tags and page/progress-section.js's loader name `pagedata/`; its links to mstar.py, pow2.py, proof384.py and proof384.wl name their new places; the reader's command for the 384 quals is `python3 -m unittest quals/quals_proof384.py quals/quals_proof384_page.py`.

The shepherd wrote its statuses to searchwatch/shepherd.log; no file reappeared at the root.

READMEs in the top-level directories (Claude, Fable 5.2, 2026-11-10; dreeves: "can you put READMEs in each directory, stating at the top that they're all you, and documenting the directory's purpose?"; `scratch/readmes-20261110/NOTES.md`).
- **What stands:** a README.md in each of engine/, page/, pagedata/, pagegen/, proofs/, quals/, searches/ and searchwatch/. First line `This file is all Claude (Fable 5.2).`; then the directory's purpose; then its files as relative links, a line each. `searches/README.md` lists the 19 searches, proof searches and witness searches, with a line each on how they ended; `proofs/README.md` has one line for count128/.

The 32 extra files and the agents are gone. Of the quals he then wrote: "i have no opinions about the README quals. i guess leave what's there and if they fail in the future we can talk about why".

Checked by running: the kept set of 2^23 numbers at 2^24 (order exactly 25, and it is the half that certificate_2^24.wl builds), the count at 128 (431 partitions, 303 antisymmetric, none symmetric, 367 up to reversal).
- **What the records say of how searches ended:** overnight_240's version 3 stopped with 315 nodes open and version 4 at 304:9 with 281; overnight_v5 closed 304:9 (601 nodes) and 240:11 (19,682 nodes; boxsplit by 17 closed 602 of its 606 open leaves and by 19 the last four); the 384:11 run kept no verdict; focus 480:14 stopped at 29,310,465 LP solves in its second case of 195; the 672:13 proof search stopped with 607 fanout jobs ended, and had no proof to find (672:13 exists); the 432:12 fanout stopped at 651,394 jobs the day the pin tree settled the cell; join720's 16 attempts timed out, and the three 288 searches and the 592 pilot came back empty.

None reads a README's words. `scratch/readmes-20261110/mutations.py`: five mutations, each caught and green again once undone.

The README's [COM]: boxes for what Figure 1 leaves out (Claude, Fable 5.2, 2026-11-10; dreeves: "ready for [COM] and [BOU] next?"; `scratch/com-20261110/NOTES.md`).
- **The page:** the two bullets above the chart are now four checkboxes (`ul#mstar-omit`) with the README's statements, math typeset: n > 0 odd (order 0), n > 2 an odd multiple of 2 (1), n > 4 an odd multiple of 4 (3), n ≥ 200 an odd multiple of 8 (7). A checked box leaves those columns out. The first two are checked as the page loads, so the chart opens as it did; no "Nothing" box, since none checked is that state.
- **Why not all four checked at load:** the chart's quals pin the default view (257 columns, the zoom's click counts, the label steps, a box per column); keeping it costs none of them.
- **The script:** page/mstar-section.js takes its columns from the boxes and asserts each box's statement of every n it would leave out; its n labels use the steps 1, 5, 10, 25, 50, 125 times the last columns' spacing (today's steps when that is 4); an odd column reads out that n is odd and its box shows the bound and n ≡ 1 mod 2; a change of a box closes the open box and shows every column. page/shared.js: `ncol` is a function for both charts, panZoom returns `recolumn`, `PROOF.odd`.
- **The README's question about 16:** every large enough odd multiple of 16 has order exactly 15 (the theorem of 2026-11-09 with Divisibility), with a threshold of 2^O(m²); in the chart the 32 odd multiples of 16 run from 4 to 14, so there is no box for 16.
- **Quals:** quals_index.q_omit_boxes_state_what_the_data_holds and q_omit_boxes_change_the_columns (red first), and q_a_box_whose_statement_fails_says_so: a box's statement is checked against the data when the box leaves columns out, at load and at a change, and a failure shows in the readout and is uncaught. The first version checked every box at load, which seven quals with made-up data caught. Suites: `scratch/green-20261109/com2.out` (quals_index 177 of 177, quals_pow2_page 37 of 37) and `com1.out`.
- **Branches added:** two, in the change handler: the box under the chart is closed only if one is open, and a failed statement is caught, shown and rethrown.
- **The n labels' steps, fixed the same day** (dreeves: "go ahead and add quals for and then fix the errors you found"): a sweep of all 16 box states at 8 widths and 4 pixel ratios (`scratch/com-20261110/matrix.mjs`) found that at 390 px the six states with the first box unchecked stopped the chart ("Error drawing the chart: Error: [298,1024]": no step left 48 px between its labels), and computing each state's steps found two states with only the labels 400 and 800 at 1280 px. The steps are now 1, 5, 10, 25, 50, 125, 250 and 500 times the least n all of whose multiples are columns (4 for the chart as it opens, whose steps are unchanged), those with two labels or more. q_omit_boxes_change_the_columns walks all 16 states at 1280 px and 390 px. One condition added: the filter that drops a step with fewer than two labels. quals_index 177 of 177.
- **The boxes moved into the chart's bar** (dreeves, after his commit 1328d21: "i thought the changes hadn't made it because they weren't next to the chart itself. i moved them. can you vet that?"): he put the list between the bar's legend and its zoom buttons, under a sentence of his. The move left the list's CSS behind (`.mstar > ul.omit` no longer matched: bullets beside the boxes, and the sentence and the list side by side in the bar's row) and turned q_prose_has_one_measure red (the sentence and the items ended 444 and 960 px from main's left, not 736). page/style.css now styles `.omit` wherever it is, and in a bar gives the sentence and the list a row each, the items as wide as the running text. Screenshots: `scratch/com-20261110/vet2-*-crop.png`.
- **"the checkboxes aren't working for me on mobile in production"** (dreeves, minutes after pushing): production's six page files had the commit's hashes, and an emulated phone with an empty cache redrew the chart there at a tap, so the likely cause is the phone's cache: GitHub Pages sends every file with `max-age=600`, and a reload fetches index.html again but may reuse scripts it holds as fresh. Not verified on the phone.

The README's [BOU]: Figure 1 to 2048, caps for its reset button, and rows that follow the view (Claude, Fable 5.2, 2026-11-10; dreeves: "ok, go ahead with [BOU]"; `scratch/bou-20261110/NOTES.md`).
- **The README:** "[BOU] Let's try having Figure 1 go up to 2048. Actually, let's try this: replace the reset-zoom button with "cap:" and then buttons for 2^6, 2^7, 2^8, 2^9, 2^10, 2^11."
- **Data:** `pagegen/mstar.py` has NMAX = 2048. Past 1024 the upper bounds are two, norm and 44 new claims of `RESIDUE`, found by `scratch/bou-20261110/search.py` with mtop_at's tests on each of the 60 columns whose two and norm bound exceeds its explicit lo (208 s on 6 workers). The lower bounds are the generator's, plus the Symmetry section's codes, which mstar.py now reads as examples (`symmetry_codes`): quals_symmetry had gone red on 1040, 1072, 1136 and 1168, where the section has sequences of order 11 and the chart had 10. 2012 of 2048 columns are settled; 32 past 1024 are open; the largest upper bound is 18, at 1920 (16 to 18); mtop 19, mtopAt [2400], past empty. The table of smallest lengths gains the orders 18 (1920 not ruled out, 5760 known) and 19 (2400, 11520). Table 1 stays at 1024 (`UPPER`). pow2.js changes in one reason: 2^11's upper bound is the chart's column's.
- **Caps:** `span#mstar-caps` in Figure 1's zoom row: his word "cap:" and six buttons, `data-cap` 64 to 2048, 1024's pressed as the page loads, so the chart opens with the columns it had. A cap is the chart's last column. A click on one runs what a box's change runs (`recolumn`); a click on the cap pressed is the reset, and unlike the old button it closes the open box and clears the readout. panZoom takes `resets`, a list (Figure 2: its ↺; Figure 1: none), and + is disabled at the closest view, which is every column where there are fewer than 12.
- **Rows that follow the view** (dreeves, while this was in progress: "related request, if it makes sense and isn't too much of an anti-magic violation: however the chart is zoomed, make there always be exactly one fully pink row, ie, zoom in the y direction automatically as a function of the x zoom"): judged sensible, one rule with no branch: rows 1 to one more than the largest upper bound among the columns in view, sharing a plot 224 px tall, so the canvas is 256 px tall in every view and a view of 16 rows is drawn as before. Before this, the first version had mtop rows under every cap, which put four all-pink rows on the chart as it loads.
- **Label steps:** a step with one label counts (cap 64 on a phone with the third box checked: "40" alone). A multiplier of 2 in the steps would give 16, 32, 48, 64 there, but it would change the labels of the chart as it loads at some zooms, which quals pin; not done.
- **Quals:** new in quals_index: q_caps_follow_the_zoom_buttons, q_caps_change_the_columns (6 caps by 16 box states, at 1280 and 390 px), q_rows_follow_the_view (128 views). Changed, each with a dated comment: 14 quals of quals_index and its two harnesses, 7 of quals_mstar, 2 of quals_pow2, and quals_pow2_page's reset click and row count.
- **Branches added:** none. Three filters: a ring, an outline or an m tag is drawn only if its row is one of the view's.

The chart in sync with the page, and [BOU]'s loose ends (Claude, Fable 5.2, 2026-11-10; dreeves, after committing [BOU] as e1c58f2: "first get the chart in sync with all the known results and tie up other loose ends").
- **1456:** the chart showed 11 to 12 there, the page m*(1456) = 11 (changelog of 2026-11-07, and the section on asymptotics, with `proofs/proof1456.py`). The Symmetry Conjecture at order 12, with Codex, and six black cells of Figure 1 (Claude, Fable 5.2, 2026-11-10 22:55 to 2026-11-11; dreeves: "can you keep collaborating with codex (aka astra) to see if you can make progress on the symmetry conjecture?", then "if you're stuck on the symmetry conjecture, work on filling in more of the black cells in figure 1 (note the scripts for showing search progress)"; `scratch/symreply-20261110/`, `scratch/sym12-20261110/`; the exchange is in `omg-we-found-other-agents.md` from 22:03 PST).
- **Codex's route:** T_m(n) ≤ S_m(n)², all sequences of order at least m against the square of the (-1)^m-symmetric ones; it would prove the conjecture. Codex proved it at orders 1 and 2. Fable's audit: the proof checks; on supports (which mirrored pairs agree) it holds term by term at order 2, by Erdős's Littlewood–Offord bound, and fails term by term at orders 3 and 4 (`supports.py`); Codex corrected two of Fable's side claims (joins of lengths a, b and b, a overlap; S = 2 allows one opposite-symmetric pair). No proof past order 2, no counterexample.
- **A new exact count:** the 15 partitions of {0..95} of order 7 are all antisymmetric (`proofs/count128/count.py 96 7`, 3.5·10^9 nodes; outputs in `scratch/symreply-20261110/count_96_7_*.out`). Not on the page.
- **Order 12 by the page's method** (`frontier.py 12 4096`; Codex rebuilt every sequence and certified the tail): the conjecture held at every length but 22. Four owed a symmetric sequence of order 12: 1104, 1200, 1296, 1392; the pinned search found one at each in under 3 minutes. Left: 18 lengths where order 12 is neither known nor ruled out: 528, and 1504, 1520, 1552, 1568, 1616, 1648, 1712, 1744, 1808, 1840, 1904, 1936, 2000, 2032, 2096, 2128, 2192, none a multiple of 3.
- **Why the proof at 1456 does not reach them** (`offclass.py`; Codex's lattice certificate at 1504 agrees): the condition on the forced class lives in a group of order 3^15 at every one of these lengths, while the class has 8 minority positions at 1504 and more after.
- **Black cells:** antisymmetric sequences of exact order 13 at 1104, 1200, 1296 (12 before), symmetric ones of exact order 14 at 1584, 1632, 1776 (13 before); m*(1584) = m*(1776) = 14, the upper bound there. Each first witness checked apart from the search (length, signs, symmetry, exact order two ways); Codex checked the first seven independently. 2015 of 2048 columns are settled. Every run went through `searchwatch/track.py` (names `sym12-…`, `w12-…`, `w13-…`, `w14-…`).
- **The page:** a Fable 5.2 paragraph on order 12 and a list of the ten codes after the Symmetry section's eleven; a changelog entry of 2026-11-11 with the six statements. quals_symmetry's reduction through order 11 now takes the section's codes of order at most 11; quals_mstar's PAST_GRAY lost 1584 and 1776.

### 2026-11-11 (Fable 5.2): 1920:17, 1920:18 and 480, asked by dreeves

dreeves: "any hope of getting 1920:18? and can you find any way to speed up the search for 480:13 or 480:14". Work and numbers: `scratch/q1920-20261111/NOTES.md`.

- **1920:18:** not ruled out. Order 18 forces the class sums mod 7 to ±(245, 245, −98, −98, −98, −98, −98) (the only two admissible vectors; f(ζ_7) is divisible by 7^3, and order 19 would need one more factor); mod 3 and mod 5 are zero; mod 11, 13, 25 and 49 have admissible vectors. The symmetric estimate under the pins 8, 9, 5, 7, 11, 13, 17, 19 is 2^91 (order 17 there: 2^273; 1440:16: 2^198). The tilted search reads 2^−47 per coset at W = 280 and 2^−38 at lattice dimension 261 with the biased positions left out of the free set (th = 0.8, W = 520); order 17 reads 2^+31 at dimension 266.
- **1920:17:** ten tracked searches for an hour (tilted, W 260 to 440, th 1.1, 0.8 and 0.7, pins to 29, with and without profsearch's pins, which gain 6.2 bits; pinned block at f = 190 and 220, pinned ap): no witness. A calibration at 1920:16, where τ₁ # p960 is symmetric of order 16: tilted at W = 200 to 260 for 5 minutes each and pinned block for 7 minutes found nothing, with tilted's sum E up to 2^37 (1440:16's witness came at 2^24.8). The searches' statistics do not carry over to n = 1920.
- **480, witnesses:** symmetric estimates 2^−84 (order 13) and 2^−108 (order 14); 672:13, the lowest cell found, reads 2^−33.
- **480, the form tree** (400 root dives per row, alpha 0.25): 480:14 kept 8.9e9 nodes; forms by class 6.3e9; by class with the want orders A, B, C of the notes 7.0e10, 2.6e10, 4.9e9. 480:13 kept 2.2e14, by class 4.1e13. The form fix and the want order do not shrink 480:14's tree.
- **Symmetric-only probe** (`fold.py`): pintree_prove.py's tree over (−1)^m-symmetric sequences, floating-point LP, no certificate. Dives: 1.5e4 nodes at 480:14, 1.2e6 at 480:13; the whole pin tree at 480:14 reads 1.4e5 by the prover's own estimate. Controls: two page sequences meet the folded rows; 144:9 closes in 16 nodes; 288:10 and 240:11 did not finish in 25 and 40 minutes (240:11 with 4 nodes OPEN, where no candidate modulus fits pintree's caps). The count at 480:14 was launched 01:57 as six tracked shares.
- **Page:** a Fable 5.2 parenthesis after the Symmetry section's sentence "none is known … multiples of 32" (order 14 now has 1584 = 16 · 99 and 1776 = 16 · 111; order 15 has none), and the ten codes' paragraph credits Astra's check of all ten. quals_symmetry, quals_index and quals_symlarge green after it.
- **A slip:** three waiters used `pgrep -f` on a pattern that their own command line contained, so they could not end; a waiter watches files, or a pid.
- **The count's outcome (05:57):** not finished at its 4-hour limit. 111,149 nodes (103,515 closed by the LP, 7,520 splits) in 20.8 CPU-hours, 0.67 s a node; one share of six finished (1,460 nodes, 2857 s); 312 nodes left on the stacks, which were not saved; 109 nodes OPEN, every one with exactly 3, 4, 5, 7, 8, 9, 11 and 13 pinned (pintree's cap of 20,000 counts all the vectors in the bounds, not the symmetric ones). 52% of the splits are on mod 27. 200 more dives each (`fold_dive3_*.out`): 480:14 mean 1.77e4; 480:13 mean 3.04e7, one dive being 96% of the sum. So the dives' means read at least 6 times too low at 480:14, 480:13's is a floor (230 CPU-days or more at 0.67 s a node), and the symmetric-only route is no shortcut.

### 2026-11-11, 06:00 to 07:15 (Fable 5.2): Proposition 4.4, and 22 columns settled by the tilted search

After the symmetric-only count at 480:14 ended unfinished (above), the Mac was idle and the black cells that no search had tried were listed. Work and numbers: `scratch/q1920-20261111/NOTES.md`, "Round two".

- **How Proposition 4.4 turned up:** order-15 searches at the six odd multiples of 16 whose upper bound was 15 died at once in the pinned search's profiles ("no admissible vector mod 8"). The reason is BGPW's Proposition 4.4 (their section 4, from Berend and Golan; `scratch/oeis/reprint.txt` line 420; arXiv v1 `#S4.Thmthm4`, read from the raw HTML): with t the power of 2 in n, a sequence of order t − 1 has |f(−1)| ≥ 2^(t−1), so n ≥ 2^(t−1); order 15 at an odd multiple of 16 needs n ≥ 32784. The pin tree, the form tree and the pinned search all had the parity coset that implies it; the chart's `two(n)` and its residue test (each class sum's parity only) did not. `prop44.py` checks it on the 137 explicit sequences (the 30 of order t − 1 have f(−1) an odd multiple of 2^(t−1)) and by brute force at 12, 20 and 28. `coset2.py`: the parity coset at M = 2 to 32 rules out no other open cell.
- **Chart:** `mstar.prop44`, reason kind `["prop44"]`, placed last among the tests checked there; six columns 15 → 14 (1200, 1392, 1488, 1680, 1872, 1968). Page: `HI.prop44` (page/mstar-section.js), `PROOF.prop44` (page/shared.js), template `proof-prop44` (a citation and math, no words). Quals, red first on the generator's side: `quals_mstar.q_proposition_44_keeps_odd_multiples_of_16_at_14_or_less`; `q_schema`, `q_hi_reasons` and `CHECKED_HI` gained the kind; quals_index's made-up data has a prop44 column (40) and a violation (136), and `q_made_up_data_opens_every_kind_of_panel` holds its box's contents. The page's quals were written after the page's code (the generator's qual was red first).
- **Searches, all tracked:** the pinned block search (25 minutes each) found order 14 at 1680, 1728, 1872 and 1968 and nothing at 1200, 1392, 1488 or at order 15 (1248, 1632, 1728, 2016). The tilted search (`tilt.sh`, 1440:16's options, K 300, pins to 29) then found, mostly within seconds of its two-minute setup: order 14 at 1200, 1488 and 1296; order 15 at 1728 and 1632; order 12 at all 17 lengths prime to 3 from 1504 to 2192 (1504 with W 240 after W 200 failed); order 13 at 1056. Every witness file was checked by `checkwit.py` (length, signs, symmetry, exact order two ways). Nothing in 25 minutes: order 14 at 1056, 1104, 1248, 1392; order 15 at 2016.
- **Results:** m*(n) = 12 at 1504, 1520, 1552, 1568, 1616, 1648, 1712, 1744, 1808, 1840, 1904, 1936, 2000 and 2032; m*(n) = 14 at 1200, 1296, 1488, 1680, 1872 and 1968; m*(1632) = m*(1728) = 15; m*(1056) ≥ 13; m*(1392) ≤ 14. 2037 of 2048 columns settled (2015 before). BGPW's conjecture at order 12 holds at every length but 528.
- **Page:** `apply2.py page` (the order-12 paragraph now says "but 528"; a second Fable 5.2 paragraph and 26 codes; the parenthesis on order 14 at 16 · odd), `pagegen/mstar.py`, `apply2.py changelog` (27 statements for the day). Quals with dated spec changes: `quals_symmetry.q_order_12_is_down_to_528` (was `…_to_18_lengths`; its control now drops every code of order 12 or more, since the symmetric 1200:14 and 1296:14 cover what two of the four first-round codes did), and quals_mstar's `past` (2096, 2128, 2192).

- **Later that morning (to 07:35):** order 14 at 1392 (tilted, W 250), so m*(1392) = 14 and 2038 of 2048 columns are settled; `apply2.py redo` put it on the page. Three pins broke on the new data and were updated with dated comments: `quals_mstar.PAST_GRAY`, `quals_index.q_example_at_1440_settles_a_15` (of `past` it needs only that nothing longer than the chart reaches order 16) and `quals_pow2.q_base_blocks_and_their_sources` (`long_examples()` reads the Symmetry section's long codes too).
